# CLAUDE.md

## Scope

This file is for Claude Code working in this repository. Keep it limited to current codebase facts, implementation rules, and verification steps.

## Current App

Tauri v2 desktop app for browsing local history records from supported AI tool clients, plus their on-disk memory and skill files.

Supported sources in the current code:

- Codex
- Claude Code
- Gemini CLI
- OpenCode
- Grok Build (xAI — sessions + memory (`<grok-home>/memory/`) + skills + provider switching + multi-account + session/project delete + migrate + official-quota (weekly/monthly credit window))

Top-level UI shell — six activity-rail routes (⌘1..6 in order):

1. **Providers** — third-party API platform switcher (per CLI). Default landing route. One tab per managed app: Claude Code / Codex / Gemini / OpenCode / Grok Build (the five CLIs) **plus Claude Desktop** — the GUI app, managed via its 3P gateway profile (direct mode only); its tab always shows (like the other apps), and install/platform gates activation + the InstallGuide + gateway binding, NOT tab visibility. See "Claude Desktop (3P gateway profile)" under Providers.
2. **Records** — chat / transcript history. Three internal panes:
   - *Sessions* — per-tool transcripts
   - *Memories* — on-disk memory files (CLAUDE.md, AGENTS.md, GEMINI.md, `~/.codex/memories/`, `~/.claude/projects/<slug>/memory/`, etc.)
   - *Skills* — `SKILL.md` files under each tool's skills directory plus the cross-tool `.agents/skills/` location
3. **Favorites** — saved per-message snapshots
4. **Search** — substring search + Cmd-K palette
5. **Stats** — KPI cards + all-history calendar heatmap + a Tokens chart (Type / Model breakdown)
6. **Settings** — Appearance / Storage / Search history / Keyboard shortcuts / About

Plus a **system-level macOS menu-bar tray** (outside the in-app rail) for quick provider switching — see "Menu-bar tray" below.

Current alignment target: data acquisition and message preview formatting should follow the official tools. UI layout, source filters, project grouping, search, stats, cross-source sorting, and the Memory/Skills views are app behavior and do not need official UI parity.

## Tech Stack

- Desktop framework: Tauri v2
- Frontend: React 18, TypeScript, Vite
- UI / styling: Tailwind CSS v4 + shadcn/ui (Radix primitives); charts via `recharts`; virtualized lists via `@tanstack/react-virtual`; theming via `next-themes`; toasts via `sonner`
- Backend: Rust 2021
- Database access: `rusqlite` with bundled SQLite
- JSON parsing: `serde`, `serde_json`
- Filesystem scanning: `walkdir`, `dirs`
- Time handling: `chrono`
- Icons: `lucide-react` plus inline SVG brand icons

Current Tauri IPC commands (71 registered in `generate_handler!`), called from the frontend with `invoke(...)`:

**Scan & detail**
- `scan_all_sessions` — returns `ScanResult { projects: Project[], records: AppSession[] }` (see "First-class projects" — `projects` are enumerated folders/entities; `records` are session/memory/skill entries distinguished by `source`)
- `load_session` — loads one entry by `{ source, id }` (the path is resolved backend-side via `SESSION_PATH_INDEX`, never passed by the frontend)
- `search_all_sessions(query)` — case-insensitive substring search across all loaded session/memory/skill bodies (minimum query length: ONE character — a single CJK char is a valid query, user decision 2026-07-17; hits aggregate one per record); each `SearchHit` carries `first_match_index` for scroll-to-message

**CLI detection**
- **The settle drain must NOT swallow install events (LOCKED).** After each burst the watcher drains ~300ms (`SETTLE_WINDOW`) of incoming events, because reading the session SQLite files touches their `-wal`/`-shm` sidecars and FSEvents reports those straight back — without the drain it re-triggers itself forever. That drain used to be `Ok(_) => continue`, discarding everything. **A package manager installs by deleting the old binary and writing the new one, and the write reliably lands inside that window** — right after the probe that already saw the file gone. Losing it left `cli = false` cached with nothing to correct it: for Codex `installed` is `cli || app`, so the tab stays visible and no downstream path re-probes, and the CLI version segment stayed gone until the app restarted. Measured on three consecutive `npm install -g @openai/codex` runs: first burst always `find_cli_binary=None`, and NO second burst ever arrived. Now bin-dir events (`event_touches_install`) set `install_settled_late` instead of being dropped, and after the drain the watcher re-probes + re-emits + refreshes the tray — by then the write has landed (`find_cli_binary=Some(...)`, `cli=true`, verified on all three runs). The re-probe reads only, so it cannot feed itself new events. Session rescans are NOT re-run, and the sidecar noise is still discarded (those paths are outside every watched bin dir). Test: `watcher::tests::install_events_are_distinguishable_from_our_own_sqlite_noise`, using paths captured verbatim from a real install burst.
- **Where the binaries are looked for** (`providers::cli_search_paths`, mirrored by `watcher::install_watch_targets`) — detection does NOT trust the inherited `$PATH`: it stats a fixed ORDERED dir list, (1) the installer env var that tool's own script honors, (2) per-user dirs, (3) system dirs, (4) the process `$PATH` as a catch-all. `find_cli_binary` returns the FIRST hit, so order is behavior. Rules that have each already been broken once — verify against the installer/source, never infer:
  - **`~/.local/bin` is cross-platform, NOT unix-only.** Claude Code's own launcher-path helper (read out of the shipped binary, 2.1.218) is `platform === "win32" ? join(homedir(), ".local", "bin", "claude.exe") : "~/.local/bin/claude"`; codex's is `BIN_DIR="${CODEX_INSTALL_DIR:-$HOME/.local/bin}"` (install.sh:8). A `#[cfg(unix)]` gate on this dir made the InstallGuide's OWN recommended Windows install (`irm https://claude.ai/install.ps1 | iex`) undetectable.
  - **Windows has NO shell fallback.** `shell_version_fallback` (`$SHELL -l -i -c`, which also picks up alias-only installs) is `#[cfg(unix)]` and returns `None` on Windows. So Windows detection is ENTIRELY this dir list + the process `$PATH` — and an installer's freshly-appended user PATH entry is invisible to an already-running process. A missing dir there means "installed but undetectable until re-login", not "found a slower way".
  - **The shell fallback is CACHED, because it's on a hot path** (`providers::shell_installed_cached`, TTL 10 min). `find_cli_binary(..).is_some() || shell_fallback(..)` only short-circuits on a HIT, so the ~1s interactive-shell spawn (measured 0.9–1.7s on stock macOS zsh — it sources the user's rc) happens for every CLI the dir scan missed = every tool the user does NOT have. `detect_install_snapshot` runs that on EVERY tray-menu open and watcher burst, so an uncached probe cost a user with 2 of the 5 CLIs ~3s per menu open. Staleness is harmless: an install in any watched dir is found by the dir scan and never reaches the cache, and both the watcher's install-changed branch and the Providers-page Recheck (`detect_clis`) call `clear_shell_probe_cache()`. Note disabled tools are probed too — `detect_install_snapshot` walks `CliApp::all()` and `disabled` is only a field on the result.
  - **Anything run through the interactive shell is MARKER-SCOPED (`providers::SHELL_PROBE_MARKER`).** An interactive shell sources the user's rc files, and plenty of setups print a banner there (fastfetch/neofetch, greetings, MOTDs) onto the same stdout we parse — AHEAD of the real output. `parse_version` returns the FIRST version-shaped token it sees, so on a real dev machine `codex --version` reported **`16.00`**: the host's RAM, because `Memory  11.56 GiB / 16.00 GiB` precedes `codex-cli 0.144.6` by eleven lines. Every such command is therefore built with `marked_shell_command` (`echo <marker>; <cmd> 2>&1`) and its output read through `after_shell_marker`, which returns ONLY what follows the marker — and `None` when the marker never appeared, since a shell that didn't reach the `echo` produced nothing trustworthy (falling back to the whole text is exactly the bug). It splits by POSITION, never by matching banner content, so any banner shape works; `rsplit` keeps the last occurrence so a banner echoing the marker text can't shadow it. Two call sites share it: `shell_version_fallback` here and `upgrade::run_upgrade`. Tests: `providers::tests::after_shell_marker_*` (six banner shapes + a 20-line all-versions banner + the verbatim real-machine capture).
  - **Claude Code's legacy local install** (`claude migrate-installer` → `<claude-config>/local`, honoring `$CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR`) is in the list even though it's superseded by the native `~/.local/bin`: it is deliberately NOT on PATH — exposed via a shell alias (`alias claude="~/.claude/local/claude"`, the doctor's own repair text in the shipped binary) — so only the slow shell fallback could otherwise find it, and on Windows nothing could.
  - **Windows-only landings**: Claude via winget → `%LOCALAPPDATA%\Microsoft\WinGet\Links` (user) / `%ProgramFiles%\WinGet\Links` (machine) — its manifest is `InstallerType: portable`, and both dirs are winget-cli `AppInstallerCommonCore/Runtime.cpp:223-234`; Codex standalone → `%LOCALAPPDATA%\Programs\OpenAI\Codex\bin` (`.audit-sources/codex/scripts/install/install.ps1:741` — a DIFFERENT path from its Unix `~/.local/bin`, install.sh:8). Grok's are cited from the LIVE `x.ai/cli/install.{sh,ps1}` because `.audit-sources/grok-build` ships no installer, so those line numbers can drift.
  - **Every entry must trace to a real installer; cite the script + line next to it.** `~/.codex/bin` sat in this list for months purely because it LOOKED like `~/.grok/bin` / `~/.opencode/bin` — no codex installer has ever written there (absent from the codex repo, from its install scripts, and from cc-switch, the reference the commit cited). It was then compounded: a later reader took the unexplained path for a legacy layout and wrote that guess down as a comment, laundering it into apparent fact. Removed 2026-07-24. An unjustified entry is worse than a missing one — it costs a stat AND manufactures false provenance.
  - **Installer env overrides are probed FIRST**: `$CODEX_INSTALL_DIR` / `$GROK_BIN_DIR` / `$OPENCODE_INSTALL_DIR` + `$XDG_BIN_DIR` (each read from the SAME variable that tool's install script reads, relative order following the installer's own precedence). An explicitly chosen dir has to outrank every package-manager dir, else a stale npm copy wins the first-hit race.
  - The watcher mirrors the list so a fresh install auto-refreshes detection instead of waiting for the next scan / tray open / Recheck, with two DELIBERATE omissions — write-hot or enormous dirs where unrelated events would each cost an install re-probe: Linux `/usr/bin`, and `~/go/bin` + `$GOPATH/bin`.
- `detect_clis()` — runs `which`-style probes for `claude` / `codex` / `gemini` / `opencode` binaries; returns `{ [app]: boolean }` for the Providers page InstallGuide gate. **Codex counts as installed when EITHER the CLI binary OR the desktop app is present** (they share `~/.codex/` auth + config, so provider management works with just the app). The desktop app is detected by macOS bundle id `com.openai.codex` scanned under `/Applications` + `~/Applications` (`codex_app_info_in`) — NEVER by `.app` name: since the 2026-07-09 merge the Codex app IS the unified ChatGPT desktop app (existing installs updated in place, so it's named `ChatGPT.app`; ChatGPT Classic keeps `com.openai.chat`). Windows distribution of the app is **Microsoft-Store-only** (no direct `.exe`/`.msi` on openai.com/download per OpenAI's own docs; re-confirmed 2026-07-26 — the winget-source `OpenAI.Codex` id is the CLI, not the app), so an MSIX package is the only install form to check (unlike macOS's `/Applications` scan). The Store carries TWO listings sharing the publisher — "ChatGPT" (identity `OpenAI.Codex`) and "ChatGPT (Beta)" (identity `OpenAI.CodexBeta`, a SEPARATE package family) — and BOTH count as installed (`CODEX_APPX_PACKAGE_PREFIXES`, stable listed first = preference order). **Windows detection is split by cost, because `probe_codex_installs` feeds `detect_install_snapshot`'s HOT PATH (every tray-menu open / watcher rescan, on the caller/main-event thread) and a `Get-AppxPackage` spawn there loads the Appx module (0.5–1.5s cold) — it would freeze the tray on every click, whereas macOS's equivalent is a cheap plist read**: (1) **presence** (`providers::codex_appx_installed_in`, the hot-path `app: bool`) is a pure filesystem `read_dir` of `%LOCALAPPDATA%\Packages\` matching an `OpenAI.Codex_<publisherhash>` / `OpenAI.CodexBeta_<publisherhash>` dir (the PackageFamilyName — publisher-hash suffix varies per signing identity, so prefix-match the `CODEX_APPX_PACKAGE_PREFIXES` array, never a hardcoded hash; the trailing `_` keeps `OpenAI.Codex_` from swallowing other identities; this per-user Packages dir is readable, unlike `WindowsApps\`) — the SAME mechanism as Claude Desktop's `msix_package_roaming_parents`, path-injected + compiled off-Windows so it's unit-tested on any host; (2) the app **version** (`providers::codex_appx_version`, PowerShell `Get-AppxPackage -Name 'OpenAI.Codex*' | … PackageFullName` — the wildcard finds the Beta family too; `pick_appx_full_name` prefers the STABLE line when both are installed and ignores wildcard strays matching neither identity — silent via `output_with_timeout`, parsed from the chosen `PackageFullName`'s second `_`-segment by `parse_appx_package_version`) is fetched ONLY on the cold path via `providers::probe_codex_installs_detailed` (the `detect_codex_installs` IPC — Providers page load + Recheck; `app_version` is `None` in the hot-path `probe_codex_installs` and is discarded by `detect_install_snapshot` anyway, so nothing hot pays for it). Detect by package NAME (`OpenAI.Codex`), never the DisplayName — Windows brands the taskbar/Start entry "ChatGPT" (shared with the unified app) but the package identity stays `OpenAI.Codex` (confirmed on a real installed package name, `OpenAI.Codex_26.707.9564.0_x64__2p2nqsd0c76g0`, openai/codex#32772). **No Windows `bundledCli` (currently)**: the MSIX package DOES ship the full CLI — `<InstallLocation>\app\resources\codex.exe` (337 MB, alpha channel, verified on real hardware 2026-07-26) — and, contrary to an earlier note here, a normal non-elevated process CAN `read_dir`/read/copy inside the package dir when it knows the full path (only WindowsApps ROOT enumeration and IN-PLACE execution are ACL-denied — running it directly fails with Access denied, while a copied-out binary runs fine, verified `codex-cli 0.146.0-alpha.3.1`). So a Windows bundledCli fallback is FEASIBLE via a managed copy (e.g. `~/.termory/bin/`, re-copied per package version) but is NOT implemented — `codex_binary()` stays standalone-CLI-only on Windows until that's built. Both paths are **verified on real Windows hardware (2026-07-26)**: the presence scan matched the real `OpenAI.Codex_2p2nqsd0c76g0` package dir and the PowerShell fetch returned `OpenAI.Codex_26.721.4979.0_x64__2p2nqsd0c76g0` — the card showed `v0.145.0 (CLI) · v26.721.4979.0 (App)`. The Beta family is covered by the same prefixes/wildcard but only unit-tested (no Beta install on the test rig).
- `detect_codex_installs()` — Codex's install forms split: `{ cli, app, appVersion, bundledCli }`, probed backend-side by `providers::probe_codex_installs_detailed` (the cold-path variant: on macOS a single bundle resolution (`codex_app_info_in`) feeds `app`/`appVersion`/`bundledCli` from one Info.plist read; on Windows the cheap `probe_codex_installs` presence scan plus a PowerShell version fetch — see the `detect_clis` bullet for the hot/cold split). `bundledCli` is the runnable codex CLI shipped INSIDE the desktop app (`<bundle>/Contents/Resources/codex`, a self-contained native binary on the app's release channel — alpha builds) — the **fallback for app-only installs**: `providers::codex_binary()` = standalone CLI first, else bundled, and every codex spawn/launch seam routes through it (the `codex login` spawn in accounts.rs; terminal resume / New Session via `codex_shell_invocation()` in terminal.rs, which uses bare `codex` when the standalone CLI exists and the bundled binary's single-quoted absolute path otherwise — the bundled branch is macOS-gated, no Windows quoting path exists). Account add / re-login disable only when BOTH `cli` and `bundledCli` are false (`codexCliMissing` in ProvidersPage / `reloginUnavailable` on OfficialAccountsSection) — in practice that state means "no codex at all" (the tab shows InstallGuide), so the disabled-button tooltip is defensive. The frontend "Copy resume command" intentionally keeps the canonical bare `codex resume <id>` (it documents the command, not the fallback path). **Tray plumbing (`providers::InstallSnapshot`)**: `detect_install_snapshot()` returns `{ map: HashMap<CliApp,bool>, codex_terminal }` in one probe pass — `codex_terminal` = standalone CLI (INCLUDING the interactive-shell fallback, since the user's terminal resolves what the fixed-dir scan may miss) OR bundled CLI. The tray stores/compares the WHOLE snapshot (`INSTALLED` cache + `menu_installed_stale`), so a codex-terminal flip (standalone CLI added/removed while the app stays) triggers a full rebuild, and `terminal_clis` reads `snapshot.codex_terminal` with ZERO filesystem work on the main thread (the probe always runs on the caller's thread: watcher / IPC worker / tray-click handler).
- `detect_cli_versions_cmd()` — invokes each detected CLI with `--version` so the Providers page can show actual installed versions. The Codex entry is the CLI version ONLY; the frontend composes the Official card's version line from both forms via `codexVersionSegments` (provider-utils) → `v0.144.6 (CLI) · v26.715.31925 (App)` (whichever installed; labels are deliberate keep-English i18n keys `providers.codexVersion{Cli,App}`). **`ProviderOfficialCard.versions` is a LIST of `VersionSegment {text, label?, latest?}`, not a preformatted string** — the string form couldn't say WHERE the update badge belongs, so a Codex CLI update rendered at the end of the joined line and read as the desktop App being out of date. Each segment carries its own `latest`; the card renders the badge immediately after that segment (pinned by `ProviderOfficialCard.test.tsx`'s badge-position test). Callers add the `v` prefix.
- `detect_latest_versions_cmd(force?)` — **CLI update check** (`updates.rs`): the LATEST published version of each managed CLI, keyed by the same CliApp string as `detect_cli_versions_cmd`, PLUS the one non-`CliApp` key `codex-app` for the Codex desktop app (see its bullet below). Fetches all sources CONCURRENTLY (`tokio::join!`, one reqwest client, 10s timeout each, `User-Agent: Termory/<ver>`) and caches the result 6h (`CACHE_TTL`, `static LATEST`); `force` bypasses the cache (Providers Recheck passes `force:true`). Per-tool source (each matches what that tool's OWN installer reads, NOT a guess):
  - **Claude Code** → `GET https://downloads.claude.ai/claude-code-releases/latest` (bare version string). **npm (`@anthropic-ai/claude-code`) is NOT the primary install** (native `claude.ai/install.sh` / brew / winget are — the InstallGuide lists npm only as a secondary tab), so npm can lag; we read the SAME endpoint the official bootstrap uses.
  - **Codex** (the npm CLI) → npm `@openai/codex` `/latest` `.version`, with `providers::codex_latest_known_version()` (`~/.codex/version.json`) as an OFFLINE fallback. This feeds the CLI segment's badge; the desktop App has its OWN source (next bullet) on its OWN segment.
  - **Gemini** → npm `@google/gemini-cli`. **OpenCode** → npm `opencode-ai`.
  - **Grok** → `GET https://x.ai/cli/stable` (bare version string — same channel endpoint `x.ai/cli/install.sh` reads).
  - **Codex DESKTOP app** (macOS only) → its own Sparkle **appcast**, `https://persistent.oaistatic.com/codex-app-prod/appcast.xml` — taken verbatim from the app's bundled config key `codexSparkleFeedUrl`. A static CDN XML: public, unauthenticated, no device/installation id. Rides the same map under the non-`CliApp` key **`codex-app`** (`updates::CODEX_APP_KEY` ⟷ `CODEX_APP_LATEST_KEY` in ProvidersPage.tsx) because it is a SECOND product under the codex tab, versioned independently of the npm CLI (`v0.144.6` vs `v26.721.30844` — comparing one to the other is meaningless). `parse_appcast` (hand-rolled, no XML crate — same call `claude_desktop::version()` makes for Info.plist) + `pick_appcast_latest` filter on TWO axes before taking the max: **`sparkle:hardwareRequirements`** (the feed URL is arch-agnostic and Sparkle itself selects per item, so an Intel Mac must not be offered an arm64-only build; accepts the `arm64`/`aarch64`/`x64`/`x86_64` spellings, absent = universal) and **`sparkle:minimumSystemVersion`** (badging an update the host's macOS can't install is worse than no badge). Read `sparkle:shortVersionString`, NEVER `sparkle:version` (an opaque build number, `5813`). Windows is excluded because the Codex app ships as Store MSIX, which owns its own updates and publishes no appcast. **Known imprecision:** the app ALSO has a *backend* appcast path (`installation_id` + plan params) for staged rollouts, so this static feed can be ahead of what a given user's app is actually offered — the badge may appear before their app self-updates.
  - **Claude Desktop** → NONE, deliberately. Its updater (Squirrel.Mac, `api.anthropic.com/api/desktop/{platform}/{arch}/{squirrel|msix}/update`, JSON `.currentRelease`) **requires a `device_id`** (400 without) — a per-device telemetry token the app stores 0600 at `<config>/ant-did` — and there is no public alternative (`downloads.claude.ai/releases/*/latest` 404s; the download page carries no version). Querying a vendor's identified update API from a third-party app is a different proposition from the public distribution endpoints above, so it stays unchecked: always `None`, no badge, installed-version only. Claude Desktop auto-updates itself and has its own "Check for updates" menu item, so the user isn't unserved.
  - Shared helpers: `fetch_bare_version(client, url)` (Claude + Grok plain-text channels), `fetch_npm_latest(client, pkg)` (`npm_package(app)` maps the THREE npm CLIs — Codex/Gemini/OpenCode), `parse_npm_version`, `valid_version` (`MAJOR.MINOR.PATCH[-pre]`, rejects HTML error pages / junk). Any single fetch failure → `None` for that tool (never errors the whole call). Frontend: `ProvidersPage` fetches this INDEPENDENTLY of `detect_cli_versions_cmd` (network is slower — the update badge just appears once it resolves, never blocking the installed-version render) on cold-start + Recheck, module-cached like `versions`; `hasUpdate(installed, latest)` (provider-utils — numeric-segment semver compare, treats an installed prerelease at an equal core as behind the stable latest, missing-either → false) gates the amber `↑ New v{version}` badge on `ProviderOfficialCard` (via the owning segment's `latest` — see the `versions` prop above; i18n `providers.upgradeAvailable` / `providers.upgradeAvailableHint`). For Codex the two updates are tracked separately on their own segments: the npm `@openai/codex` latest → CLI segment, the desktop app's appcast latest (`codex-app` key) → App segment, since they're separately-versioned products. Tests: `updates::tests::*` (parsing/mapping — live fetches untested, same policy as quota) + `provider-utils.test.ts` (`hasUpdate`, plus segment/`latest` placement) + `ProviderOfficialCard.test.tsx` (badge shown/hidden + positioned after its own segment).
- **Codex: "which version is installed" and "can a codex be run" are SEPARATE questions (LOCKED).** `providers::codex_standalone_cli_installed()` (`find_cli_binary` only) answers the VERSION side: is there a real binary the user installed and can upgrade themselves. `providers::codex_cli_installed()` (that OR the interactive-shell probe) answers the EXECUTION side. The probes themselves are unchanged — what matters is not merging their results with `||` and handing one bool to everything. `CodexInstalls.cli` takes the narrow one, so the CLI version segment, its version number, and its upgrade badge all describe the standalone product only; the desktop app's bundled copy (`<bundle>/Contents/Resources/codex`, an alpha-channel build that updates with the app) can never masquerade as an install. Tab visibility, `codex_terminal`, `codex_binary()` and the `codex login` spawn take the wide one and DO fall back to the bundled copy, so an app-only install keeps working. The shell probe belongs to execution because it only proves "some codex answered", never WHICH — using it on the version side once made the CLI segment describe a binary the user never installed.
- `detect_terminals()` — lists the mainstream terminals installed on this OS (+ "auto") for the Settings → Terminal dropdown (`terminal::detect`)
- `resume_session_in_terminal(source, id, project)` — opens a session in the chosen terminal and resumes it (`terminal::resume_session`); driven by the Records / Favorites right-click menu, same path as the tray's recent-session click. Returns `Result<(), String>` so a launch failure (terminal missing / spawn error) surfaces as a toast in the right-click flow; the tray's three terminal launches (`resume_session` + both `new_session` entries) discard it with `let _ =` — no toast, no log, deliberately. They only hand a command to a terminal, and a failure is visible in that terminal; the 22 other `log::error!`/`warn!` sites in tray.rs guard writes to config or credentials, where a silent failure would leave no trace at all. Do not "fix" the asymmetry by adding logging here. **The launch goes through `process::spawn_detached`**, which reaps the child but never kills it — the terminal must outlive Termory (see "Subprocess management"); dropping the `Child` here used to leave a zombie per launch
- `new_session_in_terminal(source, project)` — opens the chosen terminal in `project`'s cwd and launches `source`'s CLI **fresh** (no resume, `terminal::new_session` — same path as the tray's New Session). Driven by the Records sidebar project-row "New session" action; failure → `menu.terminalError` toast

**CLI upgrade (`src-tauri/src/upgrade.rs`)** — driven by the Providers card's update badge.
- `cli_upgrade_commands()` — every app's upgrade command keyed by the `CliApp` string (same map shape as `detect_cli_versions_cmd`). Apps with no command-line upgrade are ABSENT. Feeds the badge tooltip only.
- `run_cli_upgrade(app)` — runs it, resolving when finished. On failure the Err is the command's own last output line, which becomes the badge tooltip.
- **Four CLIs ship their own upgrade command, used VERBATIM** — `claude update` (main.tsx:6160), `codex update` (cli/src/main.rs:799), `opencode upgrade` (cli/cmd/upgrade.ts), `grok update` (shipped `--help`, 0.2.111). **Codex's is the BARE `codex update`, never `terminal::codex_shell_invocation()`** — that helper falls back to the desktop app's bundled binary BY ABSOLUTE PATH, which is right for LAUNCHING a session (any codex will do) and wrong for upgrading (only a self-managed install can upgrade itself; codex classifies the bundled copy as `InstallMethod::Other` and bails with "Could not detect the Codex installation method", cli/src/main.rs:809). Borrowing it once put `'/Applications/ChatGPT.app/Contents/Resources/codex' update` in the badge tooltip. Pinned by `upgrade::tests::upgrade_commands_never_reference_a_bundled_binary`, which rejects any absolute path or `.app/` segment in ANY app's command (verified to fail against the old form on a host with no standalone codex). Each detects its own install method using signals unreadable from outside (Codex keys off the `CODEX_MANAGED_BY_NPM` env var its npm shim sets — install-context/src/lib.rs:110-112 — which lives in codex's environment, not ours). Do NOT replace these with a Termory-inferred package-manager command.
- **Gemini is the only app needing inference** — it has no update subcommand (verified against `gemini --help`). `install_method_from_path` reads the CANONICALIZED binary path: `/Cellar/` or a macOS `/opt/homebrew`|`/usr/local` prefix → Brew (mirrors Codex's own check, install-context/src/lib.rs:220); `/.bun/` → Bun and `/pnpm/` → Pnpm, both tested BEFORE the generic `/node_modules/` → Npm arm because their global roots also contain that segment. No match → npm (Gemini's primary distribution). Command forms follow Codex's `UpdateAction::command_args` (tui/src/update_action.rs:41-43) — bare `install -g <pkg>`, no `@latest` pin — except pnpm, whose global add is `add -g`.
- **The run goes through `$SHELL -l -i -c` (LOCKED)**, the same form as `providers::shell_version_fallback`. A GUI process inherits launchd's bare PATH (`/usr/bin:/bin:/usr/sbin:/sbin`) — measured on macOS, `npm`/`brew`/all five CLIs are absent. That matters beyond finding the CLI itself: `codex update` spawns `npm install -g @openai/codex`, and that npm lives in nvm/volta. **`-l` alone is NOT enough** — zsh reads `.zshrc` only when INTERACTIVE, and that's where nvm is set up; verified both ways from a bare env.
- **stdin AND stderr are both `Stdio::null()` (LOCKED).** stdin: `opencode upgrade` prompts when it can't identify the install method (cli/cmd/upgrade.ts:33-45) and `claude update` can want a sudo password (utils/autoUpdater.ts:498) — with no stdin those exit non-zero instead of hanging. stderr: the command's own stderr is already folded in by the `2>&1`; what's left on fd 2 is the interactive shell's rc-time noise, which nothing reads — and **a piped stream with no reader deadlocks** once the buffer fills (~64 KB; reproduced directly: a child writing 400 KB to an unread piped stderr never returns its stdout).
- **`START_MARKER` strips rc noise (don't remove).** An interactive shell sources rc files, and many `.zshrc`s print a banner onto the same stdout we read. The shell echoes `__termory_upgrade_start__` before the command; the reader drops everything up to it. Without it a FAILURE gets reported as "Disk 330 GiB / 460 GiB" instead of the real reason.
- **The output reader takes BYTES and decodes LOSSILY — never `lines()` (LOCKED).** `drain_marked_output` (split out of `run_upgrade` so tests drive the real reader) does `read_until(b'\n')` + `String::from_utf8_lossy`. `AsyncBufReadExt::lines()` yields `io::Result<String>` and returns **`Err` for a line that isn't valid UTF-8**, which the `while let Ok(..)` consuming it read as end-of-stream. That is not an edge case on Windows: a console child writes in the system's OEM code page, so on any non-English install the CLI's own error text is not UTF-8 — measured on a zh-CN Win11 box, `'x' is not recognized` arrives as CP936 (`178 187 202 199 …` = 不是内部或外部命令) and decoding stopped dead on it. The cost was not mangled characters but **everything after that line**: the failure tooltip came up EMPTY (a bare exit code for a command that had explained itself), and the drain stopped while the child kept writing — the same unread-pipe deadlock the stderr bullet above describes, turning a chatty failure into a hang until `UPGRADE_TIMEOUT` (10 min). Lossy decoding cannot fail, so the drain always reaches EOF and the ASCII skeleton (command names, paths, versions, error codes) survives any code page. Tests: `the_drain_survives_a_line_that_is_not_utf8` (fed non-UTF-8 bytes directly, so it pins the rule on every platform) + `windows_upgrade_child_reports_a_failure_in_any_code_page`.
- **The Windows child is a DIFFERENT shell form and is tested as one.** `cmd /C "echo <marker>& <cmd> 2>&1"` — cmd.exe has no rc file, so nothing precedes the marker, but it is echoed anyway so the reader's filter is identical on both paths. `windows_upgrade_child_{emits_the_marker_before_the_output,reports_a_failure_in_any_code_page}` drive a real child through the real reader (the unix form's marker handling is covered by `providers::tests::after_shell_marker_*`).
- **Frontend — one pure function decides the badge, the component only renders it.** `upgradeBadgeState(segment, {upgrading, error, canUpgrade})` (provider-utils) returns `{label, tone, clickable, disabled, tooltip}`; `UpgradeBadge` maps that to classes and copy with no logic of its own. Four states: **amber `↑ New v1.2.3`** idle, click upgrades (tooltip: the command) · **amber `↑ Upgrading`** running — same badge, LABEL swapped, `disabled` so a second click can't start a parallel run (**no spinner**, user decision 2026-07-26; the disabled state carries only a light `opacity-80` — the label already says "Upgrading", so the fade is a hint rather than the signal, and a heavier one made the badge the user is watching hard to read) · **red `↑ New v1.2.3`** last attempt failed, still clickable to retry, tooltip = the reason + `providers.upgradeRunItYourself` + the command · **amber, not a button** for a segment with no `upgradeCommand`. While an upgrade runs the badge renders **no tooltip element at all** (`tooltip: "none"` → `UpgradeBadge` returns the bare badge, not a `<Tooltip>`). Two reasons: a `disabled` button dispatches no hover events so a fresh one can't open anyway, and a tooltip left OPEN from before the click would keep rendering the command — which is re-probed mid-run, and mid-reinstall Codex briefly resolves to the desktop app's absolute path, flashing `/Applications/...` at the user. Pinned by `ProviderOfficialCard.test.tsx`'s "renders no tooltip while an upgrade runs".
- **The card's layout NEVER changes (LOCKED)** — no progress row, no error row; every state rides on the badge. **The terminal is only ever TEXT in the failure tooltip, never a button anywhere** (LOCKED): there is no one-click terminal launch and no terminal IPC; failures needing a human are run by the user themselves. The red state is deliberately redundant with the failure toast — it PERSISTS after the toast dismisses; a retry clears it back to amber.
- **`upgrading`/`upgradeError` are per-APP, the badge is per-SEGMENT.** `upgradeBadgeState` takes a SEGMENT and treats one without a command as display-only, ignoring app state entirely — the Codex rule made structural (it renders CLI + App; only the CLI is upgradable, the desktop app self-updates via Sparkle). `VersionSegment.upgradeCommand` sits alongside `latest`, which was already per-segment for the same reason; `codexVersionSegments` takes `cliUpgradeCommand` exactly like its existing `cliLatest`/`appLatest` split.
- **`cli_upgrade_commands` must NOT join `refreshVersions`'s `Promise.all` (LOCKED).** It's a separate `refreshUpgradeCommands` callback with its own catch, fired via `void` from `refreshVersions` — same triggers (Gemini's command derives from the binary path, so an install change must re-derive it) but an independent promise. This follows the rule `refreshLatestVersions` states in its own comment: a secondary fetch must never block or blank the installed-version render. Coupling them once already made a failed upgrade-command fetch blank the whole version line.
- `onUpgrade` takes **no arguments** — the backend derives what to run from the app alone, so passing a command would imply a choice the caller doesn't have.
- The success toast (`providers.upgradeDone`) carries **no version number**: for Codex "updated to vX" is ambiguous across its two segments, and the refreshed version is already on the card.
- Tests: `upgrade::tests::*` (path inference incl. Windows-shaped paths + the macOS-only `/usr/local` rule); `provider-utils.test.ts` table-drives all six `upgradeBadgeState` branches (verified to fail when the segment gate is removed) + the CLI-segment-only command; `ProviderOfficialCard.test.tsx` covers RENDERING only (+ the locked row count, + Codex's two segments) and needs a local `ResizeObserver` shim, since clicking a badge hovers it and mounts the Radix tooltip. No test inspects tooltip CONTENTS — Radix's floating content doesn't render under jsdom.

**Records migration & deletion (see "First-class projects, migration & deletion")** — frontend sends `(project, rel)`, never a raw path
- `migrate_claude_project(old_path, new_path, delete_old)` — move a renamed project's whole slug dir (sessions + companion `<id>/` dirs + auto-memory) into the new path's slug dir; returns `ClaudeMigrationResult { sessions, memory_files, old_dir, new_dir, new_project }`
- `migrate_claude_session(project, rel, new_path, delete_old)` / `migrate_claude_memory(project, rel, new_path, delete_old)` — migrate one session (+ companion dir) / one auto-memory; located by `project + rel`
- `migrate_codex_session(id, new_path)` / `migrate_codex_project(project, new_path)` — re-point a Codex session / a whole cwd's sessions to `new_path` by rewriting the rollout `payload.cwd` (authoritative — the `threads` table is a backfill cache) + `UPDATE threads.cwd`; returns `CodexMigrationResult { sessions, new_project }`. Pure metadata rewrite (no file move — Codex rollout files stay in `~/.codex/sessions/`). Project form includes archived. See "Migrate (Claude + Codex + Gemini)".
- `migrate_gemini_project(project, new_path)` — re-point a Gemini project's history to `new_path` by rewriting its `.project_root` ownership markers (tmp + history base dirs); gemini self-heals its `projects.json` registry from the markers on next run, so no file move + no registry write. Project-level only (Gemini sessions are project-scoped). Returns `GeminiMigrationResult { sessions, new_project }`. See "Migrate (Claude + Codex + Gemini + OpenCode)".
- `migrate_opencode_session(id, new_path)` / `migrate_opencode_project(project, new_path)` — re-point an OpenCode session / a whole cwd's sessions to `new_path` via `UPDATE session SET directory = ?, path = NULL` (by id / by directory); `project_id` left intact (no empty-project resurrection, no registry write). Returns `OpencodeMigrationResult { sessions, new_project }`. Regrouping convenience (resume-by-id works across renames). See "Migrate (…)".
- `migrate_grok_session(id, new_path)` / `migrate_grok_project(project, new_path)` — MOVE a Grok session / a whole cwd's session dirs into the destination cwd's `encode_cwd_dirname`-derived dir (`fs::rename`) + rewrite each `summary.json` info.cwd. Grok resumes per-cwd by scanning the encoded dir, so files physically move (paths change → `old_dir`/`new_dir` in the result for the frontend remap). Returns `GrokMigrationResult { sessions, new_project, old_dir, new_dir }`. See "Migrate (…)".
- `delete_claude_project(project)` — `remove_dir_all(~/.claude/projects/<sanitize(project)>)` (never touches the cwd)
- `delete_claude_session(project, rel)` / `delete_claude_memory(project, rel)` — delete one record, path built from `project + rel` + `is_safe_rel`
- `delete_gemini_project(project)` / `delete_gemini_session(project, rel)` / `delete_gemini_memory(project, rel)` — same, under `~/.gemini/tmp/<id>/` matched by `.project_root`.
- `delete_codex_session(id)` / `delete_codex_project(project)` — **real delete of sqlite rows by id/cwd + the rollout files**. Codex's `threads` table is a CACHE rebuilt from rollout files (see codex_follow.rs), so deleting only the row is reverted by backfill — we `fs::remove_file` each `rollout_path` FIRST, then `DELETE FROM threads WHERE id|cwd = ?`. **File-first is deliberate:** a crash after the committed DELETE but before file removal would let backfill resurrect the row; removing the authoritative file first means a crash leaves only a harmless re-deletable orphan row, never a resurrection. Project delete removes ALL of a cwd's threads INCLUDING archived ones (user-confirmed "delete = full"). `delete_codex_memory(rel)` removes one `.md` under `~/.codex/memories/` (bounded by `is_safe_rel`; Termory never reads `memories_1.sqlite`, so nothing to sync).
- `delete_opencode_session(id)` / `delete_opencode_project(project)` — `DELETE FROM session WHERE id` / `DELETE FROM project WHERE worktree` with `PRAGMA foreign_keys = ON` so the `ON DELETE CASCADE` to message/part (and project→session) fires; the session table is a durable primary store (NOT a replayed projection — verified OpenCode never replays events on startup), so a direct DELETE is permanent. Session delete also removes the session's **sub-agent children recursively** (`opencode_session_tree` walks the `parent_id` tree — `parent_id` has NO FK cascade, so we mirror the official `session.remove()` which recurses into `children()`). ALSO clears the `event`/`event_sequence` log for every removed aggregate (`clean_opencode_events`, skipped on older DBs without those tables) — mirrors `sync.remove()`. OpenCode has **no deletable memory** (its memory is the user's own AGENTS.md/CLAUDE.md project + global instruction files, never touched). All Codex/OpenCode DB writes open RW with a 1.5s `busy_timeout` and surface "database is locked — quit the running {app}" (`map_db_locked`) when the app holds the lock. For Codex this means either the CLI or the Codex app (they share auth and state). Frontend sends `{ id }` / `{ project }` / `{ rel }` (NOT `(project, rel)` — these are DB rows, not files); wired in `ListItemMenu.tsx` (session + Codex memory) and the sidebar project row in `App.tsx`. **Local list update keys by `sessionKey` (`source:path:id`), NOT `path`:** DB-backed sources (OpenCode) share ONE db-file path across all sessions, so a path-keyed `removeRecordsLocally`/tombstone (`App.tsx`) wrongly cleared the whole list and the deleted rows reappeared on rescan — the id in `sessionKey` disambiguates. Tests: `sessions::tests::delete_codex_*` / `delete_opencode_*`.
- `delete_grok_session(id)` / `delete_grok_project(project)` — Grok is FILE-based (dir-per-session). `delete_grok_session` resolves the session's `<grok-home>/sessions/<encoded-cwd>/<uuid>/` dir via the scan path index (the scanned log file — `chat_history.jsonl` or `updates.jsonl` — → parent), bounded to the grok sessions root, and `remove_dir_all`s it — the SAME local removal as the official `JsonlStorageAdapter::delete_session` (jsonl/mod.rs:1197, NotFound-tolerant). It then mirrors the official delete's second step (`delete_session_history` → `notify_session_updated` → `delete_doc`, persistence.rs:2458 / search_fts.rs:286): `evict_grok_search_docs` runs `DELETE FROM session_docs WHERE session_id = ?` against the ONE GLOBAL `<sessions-root>/session_search.sqlite` (FTS5 follows via the schema's AFTER DELETE trigger — "callers only touch session_docs"). Eviction is BEST-EFFORT (locked/missing DB → log-only): the index is a self-healing derived cache (per-session refresh drops missing docs; `reindex_all` prunes orphans). The official delete's THIRD step — removing the cloud writeback copy via `BackendClient::delete_session_data` — is deliberately NOT done: Termory manages local data only, and deleting a local record ≠ intent to destroy the cloud copy (a synced session may still show as `remote` in grok's own list). `delete_grok_project` removes every `<encoded-cwd>` dir whose sessions record that cwd (`info.cwd`, authoritative — the encoded dir name is never decoded) and evicts each removed uuid's search doc (dir names ARE session ids; the index lives at the sessions ROOT, so it does NOT die with the cwd dir); the cwd's OWN skills under `<cwd>/.grok/skills` are NOT touched (grok delete only removes session history). `migrate_grok_session(id, new_path)` / `migrate_grok_project(project, new_path)` MOVE the `<id>/` session dir(s) into the destination cwd's `encode_cwd_dirname`-derived dir (`fs::rename`) + rewrite each `summary.json` info.cwd — grok resumes per-cwd by scanning that encoded dir, so the files must physically move; returns `GrokMigrationResult { sessions, new_project, old_dir, new_dir }` (see "Migrate (…+ Grok)"). `delete_grok_memory(rel)` removes one `.md` under `<grok-home>/memory/` ($GROK_HOME-aware, bounded by `is_safe_rel`) — Grok's cross-session memory is human-readable Markdown on disk (xai-grok-memory `storage.rs:58` = `grok_home().join("memory")`; global `.md` at the root, workspace-scoped under `{project-slug}-{blake3_hash8}/` subdirs, `storage.rs:24-31`), so it mirrors `delete_codex_memory` (a plain `fs::remove_file`). **There IS a per-workspace `index.sqlite`** (RAG chunks + FTS, `storage.rs:110`) but Termory deliberately does NOT evict from it — unlike the grok SESSION delete (which clears `session_search.sqlite`), grok's memory index has a **first-party self-heal**: `xai-grok-memory/watcher.rs:3-9` runs a live fs watcher on `<grok-home>/memory/` that calls `MemoryIndex::delete_path` on a `.md` REMOVE event, and `grok memory reindex`/`doctor` prune orphaned chunks (`index.rs:1182`, official regression test). So a Termory-side delete is reconciled by grok itself on next run; replicating the more complex memory-index schema here would be higher-risk for no durability gain (session-search has no such watcher, which is why THAT one is evicted). `$GROK_HOME` is a real official override (`xai-grok-config/paths.rs:35` — env var first, else `~/.grok`), so the frontend detection is $GROK_HOME-robust by design (below). Frontend: `ListItemMenu.tsx` (session, `delete_grok_session`; auto-memory `isGrokAutoMemory` — unlike the marker-based Claude/Gemini/Codex memory branches this is **$GROK_HOME-robust**: a grok memory record's `project` IS the `<grok-home>/memory` root (the scan passes it verbatim), so the rel is `relUnderRoot(project, path)` regardless of where grok-home lives, and the `"grok"` tool tag — passed as `memoryTags` ONLY from the Memories pane, and grok never tags a project-instruction file — identifies it without a hardcoded `.grok` path → `delete_grok_memory`) + the sidebar project row in `App.tsx` (`delete_grok_project` + `migrate_grok_project`). Tests: `sessions::tests::grok_delete_session_and_project_scoped`, `delete_grok_memory_in_removes_md_and_guards_path`, frontend `ListItemMenu.test.tsx` (default + custom-$GROK_HOME rel) + `session-utils.test.ts` (`relUnderRoot`).
- `claude_project_registered(path)` — **read-only** check of `~/.claude.json` `projects` for `path` (canonicalizes first). The migrate confirm uses it: when the target project isn't registered, the confirm dialog adds a note (`menu.migrateConfirmUnregistered`) that `claude --resume` (command line) won't list the moved sessions until the user opens Claude in that dir once (registration happens on entry, not from the migrated files; `/resume` inside a running Claude shows them regardless). Termory **never writes** `~/.claude.json` (Claude's live config — a concurrent write would clash).

**Providers — switch CLI to a third-party platform**
- `provider_active_state(app, providers)` / `provider_active_states(providers)` — reverse-derive which Provider is currently active by reading the CLI's live config files; nothing about "active" is stored backend-side
- `activate_provider(provider, providersForApp)` / `deactivate_provider(app, providersForApp)` — materialize a Provider into the CLI's live config (or clear Termory-injected fields)
- `delete_provider(provider)` — remove a stored Provider AND deactivate it if it was the active one
- `set_default_provider(provider)` — promote an already-enabled MULTI-SLOT provider (OpenCode or Grok) to its CLI's startup default; dispatches via `providers::set_default` (OpenCode → top-level `model` in opencode.json; Grok → `models.default` in config.toml). Single-slot CLIs set their default implicitly on activate
- `take_pending_tray_switch()` — TAKE-ONCE claim of the provider switch the menu-bar tray deferred to the page (a Codex official↔custom switch, which needs the "follow sessions?" prompt a native menu can't host). Returns `{ app, providerId }` (`providerId: null` = Official) or `None`. See "Codex switch handover" under Menu-bar tray
- `test_provider_api(provider)` — connectivity probe to the provider's base URL (returns `{ ok, status, latencyMs, message }`)
- `fetch_provider_models(provider)` — hits `/v1/models` (or `/v1beta/models?key=` for Gemini) and returns the available model ids for the editor's autocomplete
- `fetch_subscription_quota(app)` — official-account rate-limit usage for one CLI, read from its EXISTING OAuth login (never writes credentials). **While an add-account flow is running for that CLI, BOTH quota entry points bail out early (LOCKED)** — this IPC and the credential watcher's `tray::force_quota_refresh`, gated on `accounts::login_in_progress` (which reads the same `LoginSlot` reservation that guards re-entrancy, so it is RAII-exact). A login flow OWNS the credential while it runs and what it leaves on disk reads as LOGGED OUT — the codex flow blanks `auth.json` to `{}` up front, claude's own `performLogout` clears local storage, grok overwrites its one scope entry. Fetching then returns `not_found`, which every layer treats as definitive: the tray row is cleared and the page hides the entire quota section, refresh button included, for as long as the user is off in the browser adding their SECOND account (reported bug — the watcher fires on the blanking write within ~600ms of the click). The IPC returns `quota::quota_during_login`, deliberately a PLAIN FAILURE rather than `not_found`, because `mergeQuotaResult` (quota-utils.ts:64) and the tray both keep the last good numbers for a plain failure and clear for a logout; those numbers stay CORRECT rather than merely stale, since every flow ends on the pre-login account (a cancel rolls back, and even a success only saves the new account to the store before switching the live login back). It returns WITHOUT calling `tray::refresh_quota`: that would stamp the rate-limit marker, and the genuine refresh triggered when the flow restores the credential would then be refused by a floor this non-fetch had earned. Test: `quota::tests::quota_during_login_is_a_transient_failure_not_a_logout` (mutation-verified — swapping in `not_found` turns it red); the guard itself is IPC/managed-state timing and is not unit-testable. Claude + Codex + Gemini + Grok implemented (`quota.rs`; OpenCode has no official subscription quota — returns `not_found`). Shared plumbing: `quota_for_credential` (per-CLI credential-status scaffold), `quota_http_client` / `read_json_or_error` (HTTP skeleton + API-error-message extraction). Claude: via `claude_auth::read_credentials` (macOS Keychain with the DERIVED service name — `Claude Code-credentials` + a sha256(config-dir) suffix when `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR` is set, `-a $USER`, timed spawn so a locked Keychain can't hang — → `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR`/`~/.claude/.credentials.json`), then `GET https://api.anthropic.com/api/oauth/usage` with `anthropic-beta: oauth-2025-04-20`. `parse_claude_usage` reads the flat top-level windows (`five_hour`/`seven_day`, + any unknown window key passed through verbatim) AND the newer `limits` array: each `weekly_scoped` entry is a MODEL-scoped weekly limit named by `scope.model.display_name` (e.g. "Fable"), added as a tier verbatim (`percent`→utilization) — so any model surfaces dynamically with zero per-model code — **plus the entry's own `group` (`session`/`weekly`/`monthly`) onto `QuotaTier.group`**. That field exists because a scoped tier's `name` is a bare MODEL name that says nothing about which window it is: sitting next to "5h" it read as a window of its own, so the label composes **`{period} · {model}`** → `Weekly · Fable` (card `tierLabels` via `GROUP_LABELS`, tray `tray_tier_label` via `tray_group_label`; both map the group to the SAME short label its account-wide counterpart uses, and render an unrecognized group VERBATIM — `fortnightly · Fable` — so a new period surfaces without a release). Taken from the API rather than inferred because this data is DYNAMIC: which models get their own window, and which periods exist, differ per account and change without notice. `group` is `None` for every flat top-level window (its `name` already IS the period) and for Codex/Gemini/grok (no such grouping); it is omitted from the JSON when absent. **It also settles `isAccountWide`**: a `group` means the API itself said "scoped to one model", so that wins outright and the id list is only consulted for group-less windows (account-wide ids, the legacy flat `seven_day_opus`/`seven_day_sonnet`, Gemini's per-model buckets). `session`/`weekly_all` array entries are skipped (they duplicate the flat `five_hour`/`seven_day`). The legacy flat `seven_day_opus`/`seven_day_sonnet` are now null server-side; Anthropic moved model-scoped limits into the array. **Usage credits** (Claude's pay-as-you-go overflow, and grok on-demand credits) ride the `extra_usage` object → `ExtraUsage { is_enabled, monthly_limit, used_credits, utilization, currency, decimal_places }`; Claude reports the amounts in MINOR units (cents) with `decimal_places: 2`, so the frontend `formatCurrency` divides by `10^decimal_places` (grok already stores dollars → `decimal_places: None`). Rendered as a "Credits" ring (`ExtraUsageItem` in OfficialAccountsSection) next to the quota rings when `is_enabled`, showing `$used / $limit` (label = i18n key `providers.usageCredits`, "Credits" / 额度 / 額度). The macOS tray ALSO appends it to the CLI's first-level row as `🟢 $used / $limit Credits` (see "Quota inline title" — `TrayCredits` + `credits_label`). The endpoint ALSO returns a newer `spend` object (same used/limit/percent, plus hard-`null` `balance`/`auto_reload`) and per-window `*_dollars` fields — deliberately NOT parsed: the account's promotional-credit balance, current balance, auto-reload, and monthly-spend reset date shown by the claude.ai WEB billing UI are NOT served to the CLI OAuth token (checked live — `spend.balance`/`auto_reload` are null, no promotional-credit field), so they're out of scope (would need the web session cookie). Codex: macOS Keychain `Codex Auth` → `~/.codex/auth.json` (**the Keychain read is TIMED via `process::probe`, and every CLI's credential read is wrapped in `spawn_blocking` by `quota::read_credential`** — a locked keychain makes `security` block on its unlock dialog, and `fetch_quota` is async on both of its paths, so these two used to be able to park a Tokio worker indefinitely; claude_auth was always timed, Codex and Gemini were the two that were not) (gated on `tokens` PRESENCE — the ChatGPT login — NOT on `auth_mode`, which only selects what the CLI uses per `resolved_mode` manager.rs:980-988; the Termory round-trip custom→Official leaves tokens with no auth_mode and the quota must survive it. Pure API-key login has no tokens → `not_found`; >8 days since `last_refresh` → try-then-Expired), then `GET https://chatgpt.com/backend-api/wham/usage` with `ChatGPT-Account-Id` (endpoint per codex-rs `backend-client/src/client.rs:296`, window shape per `rate_limit_window_snapshot.rs`); `rate_limit.{primary,secondary}_window` map to `five_hour`/`seven_day` via `window_seconds_to_tier_name` so the same labels/glyphs apply; returns `SubscriptionQuota { app, credentialStatus, success, tiers: [{name, utilization, resetsAt}], plan?, extraUsage?, error?, queriedAt }` — `plan` is the subscription tier display name (Claude: `subscriptionType` in the credentials file, auth.ts:1225; Codex: `plan_type` in the usage response, protocol/src/account.rs; Gemini: a plan keyword extracted from loadCodeAssist `paidTier.name ?? currentTier.name` (official precedence, setup.ts:221 — Google One AI Pro reports `currentTier: standard-tier` with the real plan in `paidTier`) (the FULL marketing string the official CLI prints — \"Gemini Code Assist in Google One AI Pro\" → \"Pro\", `gemini_tier_keyword`), else the id; tidied by `display_plan`, e.g. \"Max\"/\"Plus\"/\"Free\"), shown as a Badge next to \"Official\" on the card and as `Official (Max)` in the tray title where tier names are `five_hour` / `seven_day` / model-scoped brand names like `Fable` (+ unknown windows passed through; legacy `seven_day_opus`/`seven_day_sonnet` if non-null). Gemini: macOS Keychain `gemini-cli-oauth`/`main-account` (keytar JSON) → `~/.gemini/oauth_creds.json` (`storage.ts:22`); Google access tokens last ~1h so an expired one is re-minted from the refresh token (public installed-app client, `oauth2.ts:76-85`; never persisted), then POST `v1internal:loadCodeAssist` + `v1internal:retrieveUserQuota` (`server.ts:263/363`) — per-MODEL buckets (`BucketInfo {remainingFraction, resetTime, modelId}`, types.ts:255-265) grouped to `gemini_pro`/`gemini_flash`/`gemini_flash_lite` (lowest remaining per class wins; card labels "Gemini Pro"/"Gemini Flash"/"Gemini Flash Lite", tray keeps the short "Pro"/"Flash"/"Lite" — brand-English in all locales). Surfaced on the Providers page Official card (gated by `QUOTA_SUPPORTED` in `src/hooks/useQuotas.ts` — mirror of `quota::SUPPORTED`) as per-window donut rings; successful results cache 2 min (`QUOTA_STALE_MS`), failures 60s (`QUOTA_ERROR_RETRY_MS`), manual refresh has a 120s cooldown (`QUOTA_MIN_INTERVAL_MS`); failures surface ONLY as a two-line toast on manual refresh (background fetches are silent), and `not_found` (no OAuth login) hides the whole section. **A `410 Gone` from any usage endpoint also maps to `not_found`** (shared `quota_error_for_status` in quota.rs): the account no longer has this resource — e.g. Gemini CLI stopped serving individual logins 2026-06-18 — so the card hides cleanly instead of toasting an error; enterprise logins still serve quota and are unaffected. Codex's free-plan `30_day` window is labeled "Monthly" (i18n); other generated `{n}_hour`/`{n}_day` ids humanize to "{n}h"/"{n}d". **Reset time + countdown** (`OfficialAccountsSection`): each window's ring subline shows the absolute reset moment (`formatReset` → `formatResetTime`: bare time within 24h, else date). ONE window ALSO appends a live countdown (`formatResetCountdown` → `formatCountdown`, e.g. "in 4 hr 42 min" / "in 6 days"; hr+min under a day, days beyond; localized units `time.dur{Hr,Min,DayOne,DayOther}` + `providers.quotaResetsCountdown`, gated by the `showCountdown` prop) — the window you're actually **WAITING ON**, chosen by `waitingOnTierName` (user decision 2026-07-25). Two branches, because "how long until I can work again" has different answers depending on whether anything is spent (`utilization >= 100`, const `SPENT_PCT`): **nothing spent → the SOONEST-resetting window** (you aren't blocked, so the meaningful number is the next top-up — a 5h window resetting at 2pm, not a weekly five days out that merely sits at a higher percent); **something spent → the LATEST-resetting SPENT window** (every spent window must clear before work resumes, so the last of them is when you're free — a spent weekly keeps blocking through several 5h resets, and a 5h countdown would promise capacity that isn't coming). **"Blocking" ≠ "spent"**: a spent MODEL-SCOPED window leaves other models usable, so it blocks nothing on its own — counting it would answer "wait 5 days" to someone who can switch model and work now. `isAccountWide(name, group)` splits them: a `group` (the API's own grouping, present only on model-scoped windows) settles it immediately — model-scoped, no list involved. Otherwise it falls back to the tier id: the account-wide set is `five_hour` / `seven_day` / `30_day` plus the generated `^\d+_(hour|day)$` forms; everything else is model-scoped (the legacy `seven_day_opus`/`seven_day_sonnet`, Gemini's per-model buckets — and Claude's `weekly_scoped` brand-name weeklies on the older shape, before `group` existed). Listed from the ACCOUNT-WIDE side deliberately — model names are dynamic and can't be enumerated, so a new model lands on the model-scoped side by default, which is correct. **Maintaining the set is the intended workflow, not a defect** (user decision 2026-07-25): a NEW account-wide window arrives with an id this set doesn't know (Anthropic spells its ids in words, so `thirty_day` is the shape to expect — it is the example in `quota::tests::parse_claude_usage_passes_unknown_windows_through`), reads as model-scoped, and is fixed by ADDING it here. Model windows never need adding, which is why the set is written from this side. (**Superseded for the windows that carry it, 2026-07-27**: the server's own grouping IS now plumbed through — `QuotaTier.group`, see the `weekly_scoped` note above — and a window carrying it is classified model-scoped outright, no list consulted. The list still decides for every group-less window, which is all of Codex/Gemini/grok plus Claude's flat top-level ones, so the upkeep rule above stands for those.) Whenever account-wide windows exist THEY decide; model-scoped ones take over only when there are NO account-wide windows at all and every one of them is spent (the Gemini shape, where per-model buckets are the whole picture). That account-wide check is load-bearing: a plain "every model-scoped window is spent" rule is wrong for Claude, whose scoped weeklies cover only some models (Opus, Fable) while Sonnet stays usable with no window of its own. Strict comparisons keep the FIRST window on a tie in both branches. Returns null until any tier is above 0 (nothing to wait for on a fresh account; the usage may sit on a DIFFERENT tier than the chosen one, so that check spans all tiers), and windows with a missing/unparseable `resetsAt` are skipped. Every OTHER window shows the absolute time only. The name it returns is resolved to ONE row INDEX before rendering (`waitingIndex`, first match wins) rather than compared per row: a model-scoped window's `name` is a MODEL name, which could in principle equal an account-wide window id, and a per-row name comparison then lit the countdown on both — the same reason the row's React key is `{group}:{name}` rather than the name alone. Pinned by the "countdown on exactly one window when two share a name" test. **Two earlier rules each got this wrong in one direction** and the name changed with them — `constraintTierName` (highest utilization: put a 5-day weekly countdown above a 5h window resetting the same afternoon), then `soonestResetTierName` (plain soonest: pointed at a 5h window while a spent weekly was the real wait); grep those names in older commits. The hover tooltip carries the zone-qualified absolute time plus the same countdown. `formatCountdown` (format.ts, `t?`-optional so its unit tests need no provider) + `waitingOnTierName` (exported) are unit-tested; the `group` mechanism is covered by `quota::tests::{parse_claude_usage_extracts_model_scoped_limits_from_the_array, parse_claude_usage_scoped_limit_without_a_group_keeps_the_model_name, quota_serializes_camel_case}` (parse + the field being OMITTED when absent), `tray::tests::tray_tier_label_composes_model_scoped_windows_with_their_period`, and OfficialAccountsSection tests for the composed label, a verbatim unknown period, group-driven classification, and the one-countdown rule. **A quota result belongs to ONE login, so an account switch INVALIDATES it (LOCKED).** The caching rules above all assume the identity behind the numbers is unchanged, and each one turns into "the previous account's usage, shown under the new account" when it isn't: the 2-min floor made the page's post-switch re-fetch a NO-OP (a tab-entry fetch almost always leaves a fresh result, so the switch changed nothing on screen for up to two minutes), and `mergeQuotaResult` / the tray's matching "failures keep the last good numbers" rule then pinned the old tiers there INDEFINITELY once a post-switch fetch failed. Both sides therefore DROP the entry on a switch before re-fetching — `resetQuota` (also clearing the module cache `cachedQuotas`, so a route remount can't resurrect it) and `tray::invalidate_quota` (called by BOTH the `switch_account` IPC and the tray's own switch handler, ahead of its `force_quota_refresh`). **Dropping the entry must also drop the rate-limit marker** (`clear_quota_marker`): the marker records when the PREVIOUS account was fetched, so leaving it lets the follow-up refresh be refused by a floor that account earned — the tray path hits exactly that, since opening the menu triggers a fetch and clicking an account row seconds later lands inside `QUOTA_FORCE_FLOOR`'s 10s window, which would leave the row cleared with nothing coming to replace it. Clearing is what makes the failure path correct: with no `prev`, merge has nothing to retain, so a failed post-switch fetch shows NOTHING rather than someone else's usage. Two follow-on rules: (1) the post-switch fetch passes `force`, bypassing the in-flight guard AND the rate limit — the IN-FLIGHT half is the load-bearing one (a fetch started for the previous account can still be running and would swallow the re-fetch); the rate-limit half is belt-and-braces there, since `resetQuota` already drops the cached entry the floor is measured from, and it only matters for a caller that doesn't reset first (both halves are separately mutation-tested); (2) results already in flight when the switch landed (the page's own IPC call, a tray/watcher-initiated `QUOTA_CHANGED_EVENT`) describe the OLD login and are dropped by `quotaResultIsStale` (quota-utils), comparing the result's `queriedAt` against the invalidation instant — safe because `queried_at` is stamped when a fetch COMPLETES, so a re-fetch started after the switch always counts as newer. A tray-initiated switch reaches the page via `QUOTA_INVALIDATED_EVENT` (quota.rs ⟷ src/constants.ts), the page's only signal that the login changed. **The whole quota block lives in `src/hooks/useQuotas.ts`, not in ProvidersPage** — fetch + both floors + the module cache + the two backend events + the cooldown clock, returning `{quotas, quotaLoading, quotaInCooldown, refreshQuota, resetQuota, quotaIsFresh}`. Extracted for TESTABILITY: the page takes a dozen props and drives 18 IPCs, so nothing about these rules could be asserted through it; the hook is `renderHook`-able (same pattern as `useSearchHits`). `QUOTA_SUPPORTED` moved with it (still the mirror of `quota::SUPPORTED`), and `__resetQuotaCacheForTests` is a test-only seam for the module cache — nothing in the app calls it, since surviving remounts is that cache's entire purpose. Tests: `useQuotas.test.ts` (17 — fetch/floor/force/in-flight, the switch rules, both events with the out-of-order + stale guards, cooldown, freshness), `quota-utils.test.ts` (`quotaResultIsStale` + merge having nothing to retain once cleared), and `tray::tests::quota_fetch_allowed_*` / `quota_marker_reads_back_what_was_set_and_clears` for the Rust floor (the marker is a `static`, so the decision is tested through the pure `quota_fetch_allowed` both call sites share)
- `fetch_provider_favicon(url)` — proxies a one-shot `<host>/favicon.*` fetch through the backend (avoids leaking the hostname to a third-party favicon service); returns a `data:image/...;base64,...` URL cached on the Provider record

**Gateways — see the "Gateways" subsection under Providers**
- `detect_gateway_apis(baseUrl, apiKey)` — probe which API modes the gateway speaks (OpenAI `/v1/models`, OpenAI Responses `/v1/responses`, Anthropic `/v1/models`, Gemini `/v1beta/models`); returns `GatewayCapabilities`. Probes run concurrently and never spend tokens.

**Multi-account management (Codex + Claude Code + Grok Build)** (login snapshots saved to `~/.termory/accounts.json`, `{ "version": 3, "accounts": [...] }` — never writes Codex's own `auth.json` except on switch/login). Each entry's `payload` is the **parsed auth.json as a JSON object** (`live.doc`), NOT a JSON-encoded string — `switch_codex` takes a `&JsonValue` (tolerating the legacy string form defensively), and re-snapshots the OUTGOING login's live tokens into its existing store entry before overwriting (same protection as switch_claude's: a rotated-away RT in a days-old entry would otherwise turn the switch-back into a needsRelogin; test `codex_switch_resnapshots_the_outgoing_login_first`). `ACCOUNTS_SCHEMA_VERSION = 3`; `migrate_account_entries` applies its arms IN SEQUENCE (each gated on the version that introduced it, so a v1 file walks through every later one — **never `return` out of an arm**, that would make the oldest data skip the newest cleanup), and `read_store` **persists the upgrade to disk** on the first read of an old file (so historical data is actually rewritten, not just fixed in memory). v1 stored `payload` as a stringified blob → `version < 2` parses it to an object; `version < 3` scrubs the provider-owned fields below. Tests: `saved_payload_is_a_json_object_not_a_string`, `migrates_v1_string_payload_to_object_and_persists`, `v1_data_reaches_every_later_migration_arm`.

**Account snapshots vs. provider switching share `auth.json` — the field ownership is LOCKED.** `providers::activate_codex` writes `auth_mode` + `OPENAI_API_KEY` there and deliberately leaves the OAuth `tokens` beside them (that's what makes an Official↔custom round-trip lossless) — which also means a live login still reads as a valid ACCOUNT while a custom provider is active. So the two features must not write each other's fields: `accounts::PROVIDER_OWNED_AUTH_FIELDS` (= `auth_mode` + `OPENAI_API_KEY`, exactly what `deactivate_codex` removes) belongs to provider management, everything else (`tokens` / `last_refresh` / …) to the account snapshot. Two seams enforce it: (1) **`read_codex_live` strips those fields — the SINGLE point**, so no `live.doc` consumer has to remember to, and nothing derived from the document as a whole can vary with the active provider (the id's last-resort `stable_hash` branch hashes the STRIPPED doc, never the file text: hashing the raw file split ONE login into two accounts the moment a provider was activated); (2) **`switch_codex` calls `carry_over_provider_fields`**, taking those two fields from the CURRENT auth.json rather than from the snapshot (absent there ⇒ removed here) — deliberately infallible, since writing no key is always safe (Codex falls back to `tokens`) while writing a stale one from an unrelated snapshot is the bug this prevents. Net: **switching accounts never changes `OPENAI_API_KEY`**, in either direction, from either entry point, and is immune to pre-v3 snapshots that still carry one. The tray's extra `auto_save_unsaved_live_account()` (it has no dialog in which to warn "the current login isn't saved", so it snapshots instead) is UNCHANGED and now safe, since the snapshot it takes is clean. Tests: `switching_accounts_leaves_the_active_provider_credentials_alone`, `switching_to_a_snapshot_holding_a_key_does_not_write_it_back`, `snapshots_never_capture_the_active_provider_credentials`, `a_hashed_login_id_does_not_change_when_a_provider_is_activated` (asserts the one-login-one-account invariant on the HASHED id branch — the only one a provider's fields can reach; asserting it on the normal `tokens.account_id` branch passes regardless of the implementation, so a test written there catches nothing), `reading_the_store_scrubs_provider_keys_from_historical_snapshots`.
- `list_accounts(app)` — returns `AccountsState { current, accounts, storageWarning }`: current live login + all saved snapshots with `active` + `needsRelogin` flags. Snapshot MANAGEMENT (save/switch/delete/add/re-login) covers Codex + Claude + Grok (frontend `isManaged = codex || claude || grok`; the three Add-account flows share the login-URL dialog and one `loginApp` lock so only one login runs at a time). **Claude switching** (`switch_claude` + `claude_auth.rs`): payload = `{credentials, oauthAccount}` — the credential doc (`{"claudeAiOauth": …}`) via the two-tier store (macOS Keychain through `/usr/bin/security` — SAME binary Claude spawns, so the entry's ACL matches and no auth prompt appears; `security -i` stdin form, 4096B line limit → argv fallback, mirrors of fallbackStorage.ts's stale-shadow cleanup rules; file elsewhere; Keychain tier is `not(test)`-gated so unit tests never touch a real keychain) PLUS the `oauthAccount` identity object restored into `~/.claude.json` — the ONE deliberate, narrow exception to "Termory never writes ~/.claude.json" (only that key is replaced, atomically, other keys + file mode preserved; identity is login-time data Claude never re-derives from the token). Switch VALIDATES like Codex: `refresh_claude_doc_tokens` (official endpoint `platform.claude.com/v1/oauth/token` + prod client_id, per services/oauth/client.ts:146) refreshes in memory first — AuthFailure (4xx≠429) flags `needsRelogin` BACKEND-side (a string Err can't distinguish a dead RT from a locked-Keychain write error frontend-side) and leaves live untouched; Transient proceeds with the snapshot verbatim; a successful refresh persists the ROTATED RT to the store BEFORE the live write (`persist_refreshed_claude_snapshot`), and the OUTGOING login is re-snapshotted first when already saved (Claude rotates RTs, so the live copy is the only fresh one). **Claude "Add account" / Re-login** (`login_and_save_claude_account` + `ClaudeLoginCancel`): the SAME flow shape as Codex — `claude auth login` is a headless subcommand (main.tsx:5747 → cli/handlers/auth.ts; browser roundtrip, fallback URL on STDOUT "…visit: {url}" → emitted as `claude:login-url`, frontend shows the shared copyable-URL dialog) — snapshot the live login (UNCONDITIONAL — see below) → snapshot originals → spawn → wait (5 min timeout / cancel kills the child) → `save_claude_account` → RESTORE the previous login via `switch_account`. Unlike Codex there is NO pre-clear of the live credential: `installOAuthTokens` starts with `performLogout` (logout.tsx:20), which is PURELY LOCAL (secureStorage.delete + oauthAccount clear, no server-side revoke), so saved snapshots' tokens survive; the rollback originals still matter because a login killed after the exchange may have already wiped/replaced local state. The OUTGOING login is re-snapshotted UNCONDITIONALLY before the spawn via the shared `resnapshot_live_before_login` (see the two-helper split below); a restore failure logs a warning but does NOT flag needsRelogin (switch_claude flags AuthFailure itself; anything else is a write error). Both login flows reserve their cancel slot via the RAII `LoginSlot` (re-entrancy guard + Drop-cleared, so no early exit can leak a stale notify). **`~/.claude.json` writes NEVER degrade a read/parse failure to an empty doc** (a mid-write claude leaves torn JSON; truncating the file to just `oauthAccount` would destroy the whole global config — same never-truncate rule as claude_desktop) and the path follows the official `getGlobalClaudeFile` (env.ts:14-26: `<config-dir>/.config.json` legacy wins, else `$CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR/.claude.json`, else `~/.claude.json`). The claude switch confirm carries a quit-running-claude hint (`providers.accountSwitchQuitClaudeHint`) — a running instance write-through-caches `~/.claude.json` and would roll the identity back; no lock exists to detect it (unlike the sqlite CLIs). The tray pays ZERO for claude accounts until one is saved: `tray_accounts(Claude)` gates on the store having claude entries before any live read (the read spawns `security(1)` and `build_menu` runs on the MAIN thread), and `claude_auth`'s 30s Keychain cache bounds the rest. (An earlier terminal+poll flow existed for one session — replaced 2026-07-27 when `claude auth login` was pointed out; grep `accountAddingTerminal` in history.) **Grok switching** (`switch_grok` + `login_and_save_grok_account`): the store is the plain `<grok-home>/auth.json` (0600, no Keychain on any OS), a scope-keyed map `{ "<issuer>::<client_id>": GrokAuth }` (`auth/model.rs:259` + `auth/config.rs:217`) with plain `email`/`first_name`/`last_name` — no JWT decode. The xAI client_id is a compile-time constant (`auth/config.rs:279`), so every first-party login lands on the SAME scope key: one account at a time on disk, which is what makes snapshot/switch necessary rather than optional. Store id = `user_id`.

**The payload is SCOPE-SCOPED (`{scope, auth}`), not the whole document.** auth.json can also hold a plain API key (`xai::api_key`, `auth/storage.rs:426`) or an enterprise OIDC login under another issuer; a whole-document restore would wipe whichever the snapshot predates. That API-key entry is kept out of account detection by requiring a non-empty `user_id` (it is written from `GrokAuth::default()`, whose `user_id` is `""`) rather than by hardcoding its key. A corrupt auth.json is renamed to `auth.json.corrupt.<millis>` before a fresh write, mirroring grok's own `read_auth_json_or_empty_recovering_corrupt` (`auth/storage.rs:181`).

**The refresh token is the whole hazard.** auth.x.ai rotates it and runs reuse detection: spending one twice revokes the token family (`auth/manager.rs:1710`, `:1823`). The blast radius is one `grok login` — the same as Codex/Claude's `needsRelogin` — but it is SILENT and arrives hours later, so three rules exist to never hand the user that:

1. **Refresh the snapshot before committing it** (`switch_grok`). A stored credential goes stale as grok rotates the on-disk one; restoring a spent token "succeeds" and then logs the user out at grok's next refresh. Validating first turns that into an error at click time plus a backend-set `needsRelogin` — the same contract switch_codex / switch_claude give. **429/5xx/network degrade to writing the snapshot verbatim** (the server said "not now", which says nothing about the token) — flagging those would trap a healthy account behind a disabled Switch button. An earlier version skipped this refresh entirely on a blanket reading of the quota path's no-refresh rule; the result was a switch that could not report its own failure (rejected 2026-07-31).
2. **Re-snapshot the outgoing login before overwriting it**, so the entry we may come back to stays in step with disk.
3. **Hold grok's own `auth.json.lock` across all of it INCLUDING the IdP call** (`GrokAuthLock`, mirroring `auth/manager/lock.rs`: whole-file advisory lock via std's `File::try_lock` plus the `PID:TIMESTAMP` holder info its waiters parse). Writing the holder info is not cosmetic — stale `PID:TS` is exactly what makes a grok waiter break a lock it should have waited on. Our hold is short so no heartbeat thread is needed, and the lock FILE is never deleted (unlinking it is grok's break-a-stuck-holder signal, not a release). `acquire_grok_lock` retries 10×150ms then fails with "quit any running grok".

**The refresh itself** (`refresh_grok_auth` → `apply_grok_token_response`) POSTs `grant_type=refresh_token` + `refresh_token` + `client_id` to **`https://auth.x.ai/oauth2/token`** (`GROK_TOKEN_ENDPOINT`). That endpoint and grant were **verified live, not inferred**: discovery lists `refresh_token` in `grant_types_supported` and `none` in `token_endpoint_auth_methods_supported` (a PUBLIC client — no secret, so the `oidc_client_id` in auth.json is the whole credential), and a probe with a junk token returned `invalid_grant`, not `invalid_client`. It patches exactly the four fields grok's own refresh replaces (`oidc/refresh.rs:255-278` + `oidc/protocol.rs:225` `build_grok_auth`) — `key`, `create_time`, `expires_at`, `refresh_token` — carrying every other field over by patching in place, the same way grok rebuilds the profile from the previous credential instead of calling `/userinfo`. **Two rules are copied, not invented:** a response WITHOUT a `refresh_token` keeps the OLD one (`refresh.rs:276-278` — the IdP rotates only sometimes, and treating the missing field as a value would blank the only way back into the account), and a missing `expires_in` REMOVES `expires_at` rather than leaving the stale one (grok then falls back to its 30-day TTL; keeping the past value would make every read treat the fresh token as expired).
