Termory writes NOTHING to config.json for grok (the earlier `grok_prev_*` snapshots were deleted 2026-07). **`api_backend` (wire API) is a dedicated dropdown** — Default / `responses` / `chat_completions` / `messages` (enum per `ApiBackend` in xai-grok-sampling-types/types.rs). **"Default" (unset) OMITS the field from the `[model.*]` entries** so grok applies its OWN default — `chat_completions` via `#[default]` + `ModelInfo::fallback` — and keeps following grok if that default ever changes; only an explicit choice is written (aligned to official logic, user decision 2026-07; the editor default was previously a hardcoded `responses`). Stored on `Provider.apiBackend` (+ `GatewayBinding.apiBackend`), written into `[model.*]` entries by `activate_grok` only when explicitly chosen (unset → omitted), and shown in the editor BEFORE the model fields. **Model-list dedup**: `reject_duplicate_model_ids` (shared — see the OpenCode row) rejects a repeat within `models`. **Grok Advanced settings (`options`) are GLOBAL config.toml keys applied ON SET-DEFAULT** (2026-07-25, reinstated after a brief removal). Everything Termory writes goes into the ONE `~/.grok/config.toml` shared with the user's own settings — there is no per-provider scope (OpenCode's `provider.<id>.options` is just a section in the same opencode.json; grok has only per-model `[model.*]` + truly-global top-level). So grok's `options` are top-level keys (`ui.*`, `models.temperature`, `[session]`, …). The multi-slot clash that forced the earlier removal was because they were applied on ENABLE (`activate_grok`) — multiple enabled providers fighting over the same top-level keys. **Fix: apply them in `set_grok_default` instead** — only the ONE default provider's `options` are live: strip the union of all grok providers' option keys (`override_keys(all, Grok)`), then `apply_toml_overrides(doc, p, Grok)`. `deactivate_grok` / `delete_grok_provider_entry` strip them (only when our provider was the default). `activate_grok` writes NO options. Managed keys (`models.default` + `model.*.<field>`, via `override_key_is_managed(Grok)`) are skipped so a user option can't clobber the default pointer or a model entry. **`apply_toml_overrides` now takes an `app` param** — it previously HARDCODED `CliApp::Codex` for the managed check, which would have let grok's `models.default` option overwrite the real default (latent bug, fixed + tests `grok_options_applied_on_set_default_and_stripped_on_switch` (enable writes no options → set-default applies + managed key skipped → deactivate strips + slots survive) and `grok_switch_default_strips_previous_default_options` (A→B: only the current default's options are live)). `set_grok_default`/`set_default`/`set_default_provider` IPC all take the provider list (`all`) so the strip covers the previous default; ProvidersPage passes `allProvidersForApp` (standalone + gateway synths), GatewaysPage passes the app's binding synths. All grok paths honor `$GROK_HOME` (`providers::grok_home_dir`, docs.x.ai/build/settings — like CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR): config, sessions scan, skills, watcher target. Do NOT touch `endpoints.xai_api_base_url` — that's grok's internal endpoint override, not the custom-model mechanism (a first implementation guessed from binary strings used it; replaced after reading the official docs). Gateway binding: `openai-compatible`, `models` list REQUIRED + optional default (`grokMissingModels` / `grokDefaultInvalid`). Tests: `grok_activate_roundtrip_preserves_config_and_auth` (activate writes entries → set_grok_default sets default → deactivate keeps slots/clears default → delete removes entries) + `grok_multi_slot_providers_coexist_and_default_picks_one` (two providers coexist; set-default picks one; delete removes only that one) + `grok_set_default_falls_back_to_first_model_when_no_default` + `grok_read_active_matches_grok_provider_not_a_same_creds_other_cli` (the missing-app-filter fix: a same-creds provider on another CLI must not be matched, else editing the grok provider never re-applies). **Quota: IMPLEMENTED (2026-07-16, source-verified + live-tested)** — Grok Build is CREDIT-based; the TUI's `/usage` data is the `x.ai/billing` extension (`xai-grok-shell/src/extensions/billing.rs`) fetching `GET https://cli-chat-proxy.grok.com/v1/billing?format=credits` (proxy default agent/config.rs:46) with the auth.json bearer + `X-XAI-Token-Auth: xai-grok-cli` (auth/config.rs:288) + `x-userid` + `x-grok-client-version`. `quota.rs` grok arm: `parse_grok_credentials` (first auth.json entry with `key`+`user_id`; JWT-exp staleness check) → `query_grok_quota` → `parse_grok_billing` (utilization follows grok's OWN order — pager `credit_balance_from_config`, effects/helpers.rs:1256-1266: `creditUsagePercent` 0-100, else the DEPRECATED `used`/`monthlyLimit` pair, else **unknown**, clamped 0-100 like the pager. **A missing percent is NOT 0% (LOCKED)** — unknown emits NO tier: the endpoint serves no usage percentage AT ALL for some accounts (verified live 2026-07-31 against a Free / `isUnifiedBillingUser` login — raw body has `currentPeriod` + the on-demand Cents + `prepaidBalance`, and neither `creditUsagePercent` nor `used`/`monthlyLimit`), and the old `unwrap_or(0.0)` — reasoning from proto3's omit-zero rule, which really does apply to a `Cent` but not to this field — gave those users a confident 0% ring that never moved even once their allowance was spent. grok reports exhaustion through a 429 on the CHAT request (`subscription:free-usage-exhausted`, sampling/error.rs:290), never through this endpoint, so there is nothing to read; showing no ring is the honest rendering. This DIVERGES from grok's own `/usage`, which prints `Weekly limit: 0%` for these accounts — deliberate, since a ring reads as authoritative where a line of text does not. An empty result shows **NOTHING** — no rings and no placeholder copy (user decision 2026-07-31, reversing a `providers.quotaUnavailable` line that was tried first): absent data is simply absent, leaving just the Refresh control. The tray already clears its entry on empty-tiers-and-no-credits. On-demand credits are independent of the percent, so a no-percent account with a cap still shows its credits ring. **grok has TWO MUTUALLY EXCLUSIVE credit models** (`credit_limit_upsell_mode`, dispatch/billing.rs:54) and both are surfaced: legacy users get an on-demand cap → `ExtraUsage` (the "Credits" ring, `$used / $limit`); **unified-billing users buy PREPAID credits → `SubscriptionQuota.prepaid_balance`** (`parse_grok_prepaid_balance` ← `config.prepaidBalance`, a `Cent` stored as NEGATIVE cents by accounting convention — grok abs()es it at credit_bar.rs:121/:188 — so `-1250` is $12.50 REMAINING; zero/absent → `None`). A unified subscriber has `onDemandCap: 0`, so before this field a bought balance rendered as NOTHING. Its card item (`PrepaidBalanceItem`) deliberately has **no ring** (a balance has no limit to divide by, so any arc would be invented) and **no low-balance tint** (grok's own warning fires only at 100% usage AND is gated on the auto-top-up rule from a separate endpoint we don't call — an ungated "$10 is low" would cry wolf at auto-top-up users). **The tray carries it too** (`TrayQuota.balance` → `balance_label`, appended LAST as `$12.50 Balance`, i18n `tray.balance`) — with **no pressure glyph**, for the same reason the card has no ring: every other segment's 🟢/🟡/🔴 reports how much of a limit is spent. `refresh_quota`'s empty-entry check counts it (`tiers.is_empty() && credits.is_none() && balance.is_none()`), so a balance-only account keeps its row instead of reading as "no quota". Tests: `quota::tests::parse_grok_prepaid_balance_*` + the OfficialAccountsSection balance tests. Tests: `quota::tests::parse_grok_billing_*` (the live no-percent body, the deprecated-pair fallback, an explicit 0 still being a tier, the clamp, credits-without-percent) + OfficialAccountsSection's empty-result tests; `currentPeriod` WEEKLY→`seven_day` / MONTHLY→`30_day` so tray W/M + card labels apply; `currentPeriod.end` else `billingPeriodEnd` → resetsAt; on-demand cap/used → `ExtraUsage` USD). `plan` = the subscription tier display name, official precedence (mvp_agent/mod.rs `resolve_subscription_tier_for_telemetry`): `fetch_grok_plan` GETs cli-chat-proxy `/v1/settings` → `RemoteSettings.subscription_tier_display` ("SuperGrok"/"X Premium+"/"Free" — config-types/lib.rs:718, snake_case verbatim, no rename_all; headers mirror the official settings fetch remote/client.rs:17-41 — billing's 5 + optional `x-email` + `x-grok-client-identifier: grok-shell` + `x-grok-client-mode: interactive`, the last two as consts mirroring xai-grok-http/lib.rs defaults; the billing fetch itself also gained official billing.rs's `x-grok-client-mode` — each request aligns to ITS official counterpart's exact header set, user decision 2026-07-18, live-verified 200 on both; the billing GET and the `/v1/settings` plan lookup run CONCURRENTLY via `tokio::join!` — independent requests, no reason to serialize two 10s-timeout calls; both `jwt_exp_seconds` and `grok_jwt_tier_plan` share one `jwt_claims` payload-decode helper; both plan sources route through the existing `display_plan` tidy helper, same as Claude/Codex/Gemini), falling back to `grok_jwt_tier_plan` (numeric JWT `tier` claim 0-6 → display names, mirror of `jwt_tier_claim` mvp_agent/mod.rs; claim can be STALE right after an upgrade, and real tokens may LACK it entirely — a live team-account token had no `tier` claim at all, so `/settings` is the practical primary). Both best-effort: any failure → plan absent, never an error. **The QUOTA path never refreshes — LOCKED (2026-07-16, learned by BREAKING a real login).** (Scope note: the ACCOUNT SWITCH does refresh, deliberately — see `switch_grok`. The difference is that a switch already writes auth.json under grok's own lock, so it can persist the rotated token; the quota path is a read-only background fetch that must never write credentials, and a refresh there would spend a token it has nowhere to put.) auth.x.ai rotates refresh tokens WITH REUSE DETECTION — a refresh rotates the RT server-side, and reusing a stale RT revokes the whole family; grok itself persists the rotated RT to auth.json under a file lock (manager.rs:64), so a client that refreshes without persisting (Termory's never-write-credentials rule) leaves grok holding a dead RT and LOGS THE USER OUT. (A probe's "old RT still works" observation was a rotation grace window, not reusability.) So Termory uses the stored ~1-day access token while valid and reports Expired otherwise ("run grok once" — grok's own refresh rewrites auth.json and the quota returns). Tests: `parse_grok_billing_*`, `parse_grok_credentials_reads_entry_and_expiry`, `credential_path_matches_grok_auth_json`, `grok_jwt_tier_plan_maps_claims_to_display_names`, `parse_grok_settings_plan_reads_display_name`.
| **Claude Desktop** (`claude-desktop`, GUI app — `src-tauri/src/claude_desktop.rs`, NOT settings.json env) | macOS: `~/Library/Application Support/{Claude,Claude-3p}/claude_desktop_config.json` (merge) + `Claude-3p/configLibrary/<PROFILE_ID>.json` (profile) + `configLibrary/_meta.json`. Windows: MSIX-virtualized — `%LOCALAPPDATA%\Packages\Claude_<hash>\LocalCache\Roaming\{Claude,Claude-3p}\…` (see "Windows paths" below; raw `%APPDATA%`/`%LOCALAPPDATA%` are unpackaged fallbacks). | **Direct mode only** (cc-switch's proxy mode is OUT OF SCOPE — needs a local HTTP proxy Termory doesn't have). Both config files get `deploymentMode: "3p"` (merge — `read_json_or_empty` PROPAGATES a parse error so a corrupt user config aborts + `with_rollback` restores, never truncates); the profile is the gateway shape `{ inferenceProvider: "gateway", inferenceGatewayBaseUrl, inferenceGatewayApiKey, inferenceGatewayAuthScheme: "bearer", disableDeploymentModeChooser: true, coworkEgressAllowedHosts: ["*"] }` + an **optional `inferenceModels`** built from the editor's model list (`build_profile`/`inference_models`: each `{id, name}` → `{name, labelOverride?, supports1m?}`, a trailing `[1m]` on the id → `supports1m`; empty list → key omitted, Claude Desktop auto-discovers). The provider's `options` (Advanced settings) merge into the profile JSON — managed keys (`inferenceProvider`/`inferenceGateway*`/`inferenceModels`/`disableDeploymentModeChooser`/`coworkEgressAllowedHosts`) are skipped, checking BOTH the exact key AND its dot-path ROOT so `inferenceGatewayBaseUrl.x` can't clobber a managed scalar. `_meta.json` upserts `{ id: PROFILE_ID, name: "Termory" }` + `appliedId`. Restore-official flips both to `"1p"`, deletes the profile, clears our meta entry, strips any `enterpriseConfig` gateway keys. All writes are snapshot+rollback / atomic (`with_rollback`). Reverse-derive matches `inferenceGatewayBaseUrl` + `inferenceGatewayApiKey` against the provider list. | Claude Desktop's own first-party login — never touched (only `deploymentMode` + our profile/meta change), so a 3P↔Official round-trip is lossless. |

**Claude Desktop (3P gateway profile) — Termory specifics.** TERMINOLOGY: Claude Desktop is managed as a **provider** under provider management — NOT Termory's separate **AI Gateway** feature (the Gateways tab). "Gateway profile" / `inferenceProvider: "gateway"` / `inferenceGateway*` are **Claude Desktop's OWN** field names for its 3P config — unrelated to Termory's AI Gateway. (Claude Desktop can also be a binding *target* of the AI Gateway feature, like any other provider — see "Gateway binding" below.) It is **not** a CLI: `CliApp::ClaudeDesktop` (serde `"claude-desktop"`) has `is_cli() == false`, so it's excluded from terminal flows (tray recent/new-session) and from `$PATH` binary probes; it's detected by config-dir existence (`claude_desktop::is_installed`) and platform-gated to macOS/Windows (`is_supported`). **Version**: `claude_desktop::version()` reads `CFBundleShortVersionString` from the app bundle's `Info.plist` on macOS (XML scan, no plist crate), with a cross-platform fallback (and the primary source on Windows) of `updaterLastSeenVersion` in `<config-dir>/config.json`; surfaced via `detect_cli_versions` like a CLI's `--version`.

**Tab always shows** (mapped from `CLI_APPS` like the others). `installed["claude-desktop"]` only drives the InstallGuide-vs-provider-list choice + activation + gateway-binding gating, NOT tab visibility. On Linux / unsupported / no-install, `is_installed` is false → the tab shows the InstallGuide (download link), activation is blocked, gateway binding is hidden, and `read_active` returns Official without touching disk.

**Editor** (`ProviderEditor`/`GatewayEditor`, `isClaudeDesktop`): **Name + Base URL (the only required fields) + an OPTIONAL API key + an OPTIONAL Models list + Advanced settings**. The API key may be left blank (filled in later) — `apply` only errors on a missing Base URL, not a missing key (like the other providers; an empty bearer is written, no `*` on the field). NO primary Model field, no AI SDK. The Models list reuses the OpenCode `{id, name}` rows (shared `providers.modelList` title across OpenCode + Claude Desktop) with Claude-Desktop help copy (`help.cdModels` / `providers.cdModelIdPlaceholder`); 1M is a `[1m]` suffix on the id (no separate field). Model ids are HARD-validated by `isClaudeSafeModelId` (mirror of Claude Desktop's own rule: `claude-*` / `anthropic/claude-*` + role sonnet/opus/haiku/fable, after stripping `[1m]`) — a non-Claude id **blocks save** in BOTH editors (`cdModelsValid` / `cdBindingInvalidModel`), matching Claude Desktop's `invalid_config` rejection of non-Anthropic models (verified in its log). `inference_models` does NOT read the top-level `provider.model` (claude-desktop has no primary model). Advanced settings (`options`) merge into the profile JSON; managed keys are the `inferenceProvider`/`inferenceGateway*`/`inferenceModels`/`disableDeploymentModeChooser`/`coworkEgressAllowedHosts` set (`override_key_is_managed` / `isManagedOptionKey` mirror).

**Gateway binding**: claude-desktop IS bindable when the gateway speaks Anthropic — `appProtocols["claude-desktop"] = caps.anthropic ? ["anthropic"] : []` — reusing `providerFromBinding` / `provider_from_binding` (both now pass `models` for claude-desktop). The GatewayEditor install-gates **every** app: `for app of CLI_APPS: if (!installed[app]) protocols[app] = []`, with `installed` threaded ProvidersPage → GatewaysPage → GatewayEditor — so you can't bind a tool you can't run (mirrors the Providers tab).

**Windows paths**: the claude.ai exe is the ONLY official Windows channel (NO Microsoft Store listing — msstore checked 2026-07-26, only third-party shells) and has shipped **TWO install forms over time**, both handled: (a) an **MSIX-packaged** app whose AppData is VIRTUALIZED — the app writes `%APPDATA%\Claude`, but the REAL on-disk location (what an unpackaged app like Termory sees) is `%LOCALAPPDATA%\Packages\Claude_<publisherhash>\LocalCache\Roaming\Claude` (verified on real hardware; MSIX copy-on-write means files Termory writes there ARE what the app reads back; MSIX here ≠ Store — the exe sideloads the package); (b) as of **1.24012.1** a plain **Squirrel.Windows** install — binary at `%LOCALAPPDATA%\AnthropicClaude` (`Update.exe` + `app-<ver>`, deliberately NOT a detection marker: it doesn't match the `Claude*` config rule) with an UN-virtualized `%APPDATA%\Claude` config dir created on first run (verified on real hardware 2026-07-26; the installer auto-launches the app, so first-run is immediate in practice). `current_paths()` resolves the parent via `select_config_parent(msix_package_roaming_parents() + [%APPDATA%, %LOCALAPPDATA%])` — package `LocalCache\Roaming` parents come first (scanned by the `Claude_*` prefix in `Packages\`, publisher hash never hardcoded), first parent already holding a `Claude*` (non-3p) dir wins, else the first candidate. (The original bug: only `%LOCALAPPDATA%` was checked — cc-switch's assumption — where nothing matches the `Claude*` rule, so Windows installs were undetectable.) Within the chosen parent, `pick_windows_claude_dir(parent, threep)` — exact `Claude`/`Claude-3p` fast path, else a single shallow `read_dir` scan for `Claude*`/`*-3p` variants (`windows_claude_dir_matches`). The `Claude-3p` dir is resolved INDEPENDENTLY (`select_threep_dir`): the MSIX form pre-creates `%LOCALAPPDATA%\Claude-3p` before any normal config dir exists (observed on real hardware), so an EXISTING `*-3p` dir under any candidate parent wins, else it defaults to the normal parent's sibling (the Squirrel form pre-creates nothing — observed 2026-07-26 — so it always takes the sibling default, `%APPDATA%\Claude-3p`). The watcher watches every candidate parent PLUS the `Packages\` dir itself (a `Claude_*` package dir appearing = the MSIX install event); a LocalCache that appears after watcher startup self-heals via the tray menu-open / Recheck probes (`install_watch_parents`). NOTE on Windows real-hardware coverage (2026-07-26): the **Squirrel form's detection + version read ARE verified on real hardware** (tab shows the provider UI + `v1.24012.1`, Official reverse-derives correctly); the MSIX branch rests on the earlier real-hardware observation + unit tests; the **3P profile apply/restore round-trip is still untested on Windows** (needs a real gateway to point at). macOS is end-to-end verified (the app accepts the profile, enters 3P gateway mode, routes to the gateway — confirmed in Claude Desktop's own log).

Reuses the generic `activate_provider` / `provider_active_states` / `test_provider_api` IPCs and the `"Claude"` BrandIcon. `pub(crate)` helpers reused from providers.rs: `atomic_write` / `load_json_object` / `write_json_object` / `mask_secret` (char-safe slicing) / `string_match` / `infer_json_value` / `json_set_path` / `override_key_is_managed`. Tray: provider-switch submenu when installed, EXCLUDED from terminal flows (`is_cli()`), `all()` orders it right after Claude Code. Tests: `claude_desktop::tests::*` (apply / restore / read_active / build_profile-models+options / **corrupt-config-no-truncate** / version / windows-match) + frontend `provider-utils.test.ts` (`isClaudeSafeModelId`, `isProviderList`, `providerFromBinding`) / `ProviderEditor.test.tsx` / `GatewayEditor.test.tsx` (per-app install-gating).

**Model inputs use `ModelCombobox`** (`src/components/ModelCombobox.tsx`): a cmdk-`Command`-based inline combobox (free-text typing + auto-fetched `fetch_provider_models` suggestions) used for the FREE-TEXT model fields — ProviderEditor single-model primary (Claude/Codex/Gemini) + the OpenCode/Grok/Claude-Desktop model-LIST rows' ids, and the same in GatewayEditor. It renders INLINE (no portal, no Radix Popover) because both editors live in a Radix Dialog, where a portaled popup gets blocked by the Dialog's `pointer-events:none` + `react-remove-scroll` lock (and Radix Popover separately freezes the Tauri WebKit overlay). cmdk filters by plain substring; options are deduped; chevron on the right, no search icon; unified placeholder. Gotcha: cmdk's `CommandInput` does NOT forward `id`, so each field is labelled via `aria-label`, not `<Label htmlFor>` (tests query by label). **EXCEPTION — the multi-model "Default model" field** (OpenCode + Grok, rendered AFTER the models list): a **restricted Radix `Select`** (NOT free-text), whose items are exactly the ids added in the list above (deduped) plus a first sentinel item `value="__no_default__"` labelled `providers.selectModel` ("Select model") that maps back to no-default. So the default can only ever be one of the provider's own models. (A NativeSelect variant was tried for its empty-option support and removed — the Radix Select with a sentinel item matches the rest of the editors' dropdowns.)

**Base URL is REQUIRED on every provider (LOCKED).** Both editors already refuse to save without one (`ProviderEditor.canSave` / `GatewayEditor.canSave`, alongside a non-empty name), and `providers::activate` rejects it too — one guard at the single dispatch every caller (page, tray, gateway binding) goes through. The backend guard is not redundant: the per-CLI writers treat an empty string as "CLEAR this field" (their write-when-non-empty / strip-when-empty rule, which exists so a field the user cleared doesn't leave a stale value behind), so activating a provider with a blank Base URL stripped it while STILL writing the API key — leaving the CLI pointed at the OFFICIAL endpoint holding a third party's token, a half-switched state the UI reported as "activated". Only Grok and Claude Desktop errored before; Claude / Gemini / OpenCode silently half-switched. Reachable only from a hand-edited providers.json. Test: `providers::tests::activate_rejects_a_provider_without_a_base_url`. **Name** needs no equivalent backend guard — it is display-only, and an empty one cannot even persist: `config.ts` strips empty top-level fields on write and `Provider.name` has no `#[serde(default)]`, so the entry fails to deserialize and `providers_from_json` drops it. The `(unnamed)` placeholder in the page and the tray stays as the render-side fallback for hand-edited data.

**Codex "stable provider id" rationale:** Codex stores session history keyed by `model_provider`. If we used a different id per provider, switching would visually "drop" history. We pin all Termory-written Codex provider blocks to id `"termory"` (`TERMORY_PROVIDER_ID` constant) and refuse to overwrite Codex's built-in reserved ids (`openai`/`amazon-bedrock`/`ollama`/`lmstudio` — `CODEX_RESERVED_IDS`).

**Claude sub-model routing (via overrides):** Claude Code's `/model` menu in 3P mode reads `process.env.ANTHROPIC_DEFAULT_{HAIKU,SONNET,OPUS}_MODEL` via `getDefaultXxxModel()` (`model.ts:105-138`). There is no dedicated UI/field for this anymore — users route sizes by adding `env.ANTHROPIC_DEFAULT_{SONNET,OPUS,HAIKU}_MODEL` **overrides** (e.g. Sonnet → `gpt-5`, Opus → `claude-opus-4-7`). Those three keys are intentionally absent from `override_key_is_managed`, so they pass through to settings.json's `env`. `activate_claude` no longer special-cases them; cross-provider switching strips them via the override-key union, and `deactivate_claude` removes them explicitly. The editor seeds a fresh Claude provider's override list with the three keys as an empty template (`CLAUDE_OVERRIDE_TEMPLATE`), and the overrides Collapsible defaults open for Claude. Blank-key **or** blank-value rows are dropped on save, so an untouched template row never persists.

**Claude 1M context:** declared by appending the `[1m]` model-id suffix directly to an override value (e.g. `claude-sonnet-4-6[1m]`) — Claude Code recommends 1M, so it's just part of the value the user types. There is no separate 1M flag/checkbox and no `with_claude_1m` helper anymore. Reverse-derivation (`read_active_claude`) matches on base_url + api_key only, so any `[1m]` suffix never affects active-state / checkmark matching.

**Test coverage:** Each CLI has an activate/reverse roundtrip test and an unrelated-fields-preserved test. The single-slot CLIs (Claude/Codex/Gemini) also have an OAuth-credentials-isolated three-stage test (Stage 1 simulates a prior CLI login, Stage 2 activates a Custom provider via Termory, Stage 3 deactivates — the credentials file must be byte-identical at the end). OpenCode never writes `auth.json`, so its isolation tests instead assert `auth.json` stays byte-identical and sibling `provider.*` blocks/options survive (`opencode_activate_preserves_unrelated_provider_blocks`, `opencode_enabling_one_provider_keeps_siblings_options`). See `providers::tests::*` in `src-tauri/src/providers.rs`.

### Codex "follow sessions" on provider switch (Codex-ONLY)

**The problem this solves — Codex-specific.** Codex tags every thread with the `model_provider` active at creation, and `codex resume` lists ONLY threads whose `model_provider` equals the CURRENT config provider (`resume_picker.rs` `picker_provider_filter` → `MatchDefault(config.model_provider_id)`). So after Termory switches Codex to a custom platform (provider id `termory`), a project's prior official-era sessions (`openai`) vanish from `codex resume`. **No other CLI has this** — Claude/Gemini/OpenCode list resume history by project/path, not by provider, so switching the API endpoint never hides sessions. The "follow" feature exists for Codex alone; the other CLIs' switch paths do nothing extra. (Verified: the installed Claude 2.1.175 binary's resume filter checks only `isSidechain`/`teamName`/`sessionKind`/`entrypoint`/`isLoopSession` — no provider/account dimension. Codex memories are separately gated on official auth — `uses_codex_backend()`, false for api-key — so custom-API sessions can't generate new memories; that's a Codex limitation the follow feature does NOT address.)

**What "follow" does (`src-tauri/src/codex_follow.rs`).** At Codex switch time the user picks which projects' sessions should follow into the now-active provider bucket so `codex resume` finds them. For each selected project's live, non-target sessions it:
1. **Rewrites the rollout JSONL's first-line `payload.model_provider`** to the target — this is the AUTHORITATIVE source. The `threads` table is only a CACHE: Codex rebuilds each row's `model_provider` from the rollout file's `session_meta` on startup backfill / resume reconcile (`state/src/extract.rs` `apply_session_meta_from_item`; upsert with no COALESCE guard at `runtime/threads.rs:754`). **Editing the table alone is silently reverted the moment Codex runs** — this was the original bug. The rewrite streams the (100+ MB) remainder unchanged, atomic temp+rename.
2. **Updates `threads.model_provider`** for the same rows (immediate visibility before the next backfill re-derives the same value).
3. **Restores the original file mtime** after the rewrite. The resume picker shows each session's time from the rollout file's MTIME (not `threads.updated_at`), so a naive rewrite makes every session read "just now" and destroys chronological order. Durability does NOT depend on the mtime (table is updated directly; reconcile re-reads file content).

**No journal / no per-session bookkeeping (deliberate).** The official bucket is ALWAYS `openai` (official subscription AND official api-key both default to it; `login_with_api_key` never sets `model_provider`) and Termory's custom bucket is ALWAYS `termory`. So the fold target is fully determined by switch direction — switching to Official → `openai`, to a custom provider/gateway → `termory`. Reversal is the symmetric switch-back (fold to the other fixed target), exactly like Claude project migrate, which also keeps no journal. An earlier versioned change-journal (`codex-changes.json`) was built then **removed** as over-engineering once the two-fixed-target invariant was confirmed — do not reintroduce it without a new requirement. (cc-switch's legacy `custom` bucket folds one-way into `openai`/`termory` along with everything else; it's just another non-target id.)

**Safety / rollback.** Opens RW with a 1.5s `busy_timeout` and surfaces a clean "database is locked — quit Codex" error (no write) when Codex holds the lock; per-file rewrites are atomic (temp + rename, original mtime restored) and only successfully-rewritten files get their table row updated, so a partial failure self-heals (Codex's backfill re-syncs the table from the files). No DB backup is taken — the `threads` table is a self-healing cache (snapshotting it protects nothing), and rollout files aren't individually backed up either (single-field atomic change); rollback is the symmetric switch-back.

**Settings → "Keep all sessions on a Codex switch" (`codex_keep_all_sessions`, default OFF).** Turning it ON replaces the prompt with silent, all-projects following on BOTH entry points: `maybePromptThenActivate` activates then calls `follow_codex_sessions` over every candidate instead of opening the dialog, and the tray does the same via `spawn_codex_follow_all` instead of handing the switch to the page — which is what lets a menu-bar switch complete without opening Termory at all. Read per switch (never cached in state) on both sides, so flipping the toggle takes effect immediately.

**UI flow (`ProvidersPage.tsx` + `CodexFollowDialog.tsx`).** Only an official↔custom transition changes the bucket (custom→custom stays `termory`), so the prompt fires ONLY then (`effectiveActiveId === null` ⇒ official→custom for `setAsDefault`/`activateGateway`; `!== null` ⇒ custom→official for `setOfficialAsDefault`). **The menu-bar tray triggers it too** — it can't host the dialog, so a bucket-changing Codex click parks the request and hands it to this page (see "Codex switch handover" under Menu-bar tray); the page then prompts UNCONDITIONALLY for a handed-over switch and does NOT re-derive the direction (the tray already established the bucket changes, and `effectiveActiveId`'s marker map is still `{}` right after the window opened, which read as "already Official" and skipped the prompt). **The Gateways tab also triggers it** (BOTH directions): `GatewaysPage.activateBinding` / `deactivateBinding` write to the CLI directly and would otherwise skip the prompt, so for a Codex binding on a bucket-changing switch they route through `ProvidersPage.codexFollowForBinding(direction, label, activate)` (a thin wrapper over `maybePromptThenActivate`, passed down as a prop) — `"toCustom"` on activate when Codex is Official (`activeStates.codex.kind === "official"`), `"toOfficial"` on deactivate when Codex is custom/unmanaged (turning the binding off folds Codex back to `openai`). The dialog is rendered once at the ProvidersPage root regardless of which tab is open. `maybePromptThenActivate` fetches `recent_codex_projects(0)` (limit 0 = no cap, newest-first, dialog scrolls), filters to projects with a session whose provider ≠ target; if none, activates directly with no prompt. On confirm the order is **activate first → toast → then migrate → toast** (activation returns a bool; migration only runs if it landed). The dialog uses the stock shadcn `Dialog`; project rows are raw `<button>` toggles with a `Check` icon (no shadcn Checkbox exists; raw rows are the project convention) and default UNCHECKED. Two actions: **"Activate only"** (`followSkip`, always enabled — switch the provider, migrate nothing) and **"Activate & keep"** (`followApply`, **DISABLED until ≥1 project is checked** — with nothing checked it would be identical to "Activate only", so the keep action requires a selection). IPC: `recent_codex_projects(limit)` + `follow_codex_sessions(projects, target_provider_id)` (lib.rs). Tests: `codex_follow::tests::*` (backend) + `GatewaysPage.test.tsx` (both follow directions fire from the Gateways tab; custom→custom does not; + delete-cleanup keep-on-failure) + `CodexFollowDialog.test.tsx` (keep gated on a selection).

**Memories do NOT need this (no feature) — verified in source.** Codex memories live in `~/.codex/memories_1.sqlite` (`stage1_outputs`, keyed by `thread_id` with an FK to `threads`; migration `0006_memories.sql`) — there is NO `model_provider`/account column, so they are NOT provider-bucketed and switching provider never hides them. The follow feature touches only `threads.model_provider`, never memories. Injection is LOCAL and UN-gated: `ext/memories/src/extension.rs:32` enables on `config.memories.use_memories` (default true) + `Feature::MemoryTool` with NO `uses_codex_backend()` check, and `local.rs` reads sqlite + `memory_summary.md` with no network — so under a custom-API (api-key) login EXISTING memories are still injected. Only GENERATION/consolidation is auth-gated (`memories/write/src/guard.rs:17` `uses_codex_backend()` is false for api-key, so phase1/phase2 are skipped), meaning no NEW memories grow under a custom provider — a Codex backend limitation, not a "hidden by provider" problem, so no Termory feature applies.

### Gateways — a SECOND, independent kind of provider management

Additive feature; does NOT touch the per-CLI provider management above. A **gateway** is ONE `{baseUrl, apiKey}` that may speak several API *modes*; you add it once, Termory auto-detects which modes respond, and you **bind** it to the CLIs whose required mode matches. One gateway → many CLIs (one key, edit once). Design recorded in the `gateway-station-provider-design` memory.

- **Storage (`PROVIDERS_SCHEMA_VERSION = 1`):** gateways live in the **UNIFIED `providers` array** of `providers.json`, discriminated by **`kind: "gateway"`** (alongside per-CLI providers whose `kind` is `"official"`/`"custom"`) — "a gateway is a kind of provider." A gateway carries `{ kind:"gateway", id, name, baseUrl?, apiKey?, capabilities?, bindings[], favicon? }`. `config.rs` keeps ONE `providers[]`: `read_providers()` returns entries `kind != "gateway"`, `read_gateways()` returns `kind == "gateway"`, and each writer preserves the other kind (`entry_is_gateway` filter). No migration from the old v2 separate `gateways[]` array — old data is dropped (per project rule). Frontend IPC names are unchanged (`read_app_gateways`/`write_app_gateways`); `write_gateways` injects `kind:"gateway"`, and `isGatewayList` requires it.
- **API mode ↔ CLI mapping** (the binding gate): Claude → Anthropic Messages; Codex → OpenAI **Responses** (`wire_api="responses"`, needs the `/v1/responses` probe specifically); Gemini → Gemini API; OpenCode → any mode (npm SDK, one binding per supported protocol); Claude Desktop → Anthropic Messages (when `caps.anthropic`). `appProtocols(caps)` in `provider-utils.ts` computes the capability-bindable set; on top of that the **GatewayEditor install-gates every app** (`if (!installed[app]) protocols[app] = []`, `installed` threaded from ProvidersPage) so a binding is only offered for a tool you can actually run — mirrors the Providers tab.
- **Detection:** `detect_gateway_apis` (backend) probes the four modes concurrently; `GatewayCapabilities` carries per-mode `ok` + a model list (for the binding's model picker). Codex (protocol `"openai"`) reuses the `/v1/models` list since the Responses probe has none.
- **A binding is a provider minus the gateway's common fields, WITH its own id:** `{ id, app, model?, npm?, models?, options? }`. There is **no `protocol` field** — it's DERIVED via `protocolForBinding` (Claude→anthropic, Codex→openai, Gemini→gemini, OpenCode→`protocolForNpm(npm)`). The own id (vs the old `gateway:<id>:<app>`) lets one CLI hold several bindings and is the activation-marker key. `options` = advanced settings (Claude seeds the `ANTHROPIC_DEFAULT_{SONNET,OPUS,HAIKU}_MODEL` routing template); `npm` + `models` are OpenCode-only. `GatewayEditor` keeps an internal-only `BindDraft.protocol` for the model picker; `isGatewayList` rejects bindings without `id` so pre-refactor data drops (no migration).
- **Detection probes each capability at its OWN real API endpoint** (POST, empty-body route-exists `code≠404/405`) — a GET `/models` list NEVER gates a capability (only proves a list route exists → false positives). Naming mirrors OpenCode's AI SDK packages: `openaiCompatible`→`POST /v1/chat/completions` (`@ai-sdk/openai-compatible`), `openai`→`POST /v1/responses` (`@ai-sdk/openai`, **Codex**), `anthropic`→`POST /v1/messages`. **Gemini is the exception**: gated on `GET /v1beta/models?key=` returning data — that path is Gemini-SPECIFIC (a non-Gemini gateway 404s it, so data ⇒ support), unlike OpenAI's generic `/v1/models` which every compatible gateway answers. The same Gemini GET also feeds the `models` catalog (`fetch_models_list` returns `(got_data, models)`). `GatewayCapabilities` = **4 booleans + flat `models` catalog** (DATA-ONLY autocomplete, union of `GET /v1/models` + `GET /v1beta/models`; gateway routes by model id, no per-mode split). `GatewayProtocol` = `"openai-compatible" | "openai" | "anthropic" | "gemini"` (`protocolForNpm` matches `openai-compatible` before `openai`). `appProtocols`: Codex gates on `caps.openai` (Responses). `modelsForProtocol` + `ApiProbe` removed.
- **`capabilities` IS persisted** (now small — 4 booleans + one flat `models` catalog): `handleSave` writes `capabilities: caps`. Reopening a gateway shows the saved bindable sources + autocomplete immediately (rendered from the loaded `caps`); `lastTried` starts empty so opening the editor also **auto-redetects once** (when base URL + key are present) — mirrors ProviderEditor's model auto-fetch, so editing always refreshes the `models` catalog and shows a current "N models available", and `handleSave` persists the refreshed `caps`. Manual refresh / a base/key change re-probe too. A new gateway with no detection persists nothing (caps undefined → stripped).
- **Gateway base URL is stored PATH-LESS** (bare root, no API-version path): the editor strips a pasted `/v1` or `/v1beta` on save, and EACH CLI's real URL is DERIVED per protocol — `gatewayBaseForProtocol` reduces to the bare root then adds OpenAI `/v1`, Anthropic bare (Claude appends `/v1`), Gemini bare (it appends `/v1beta`). Detection mirrors this (`detect_gateway_apis` builds every probe URL from the stripped `root`; `join_v1` was removed). So a base entered as `…/v1` no longer breaks the Gemini path.
- **Activation reuses the existing path:** `providerFromBinding(gateway, binding)` (provider-utils) synthesizes the normal `Provider` shape — id = `binding.id`, base URL derived per the DERIVED protocol (`gatewayBaseForProtocol`), OpenCode `npm` + `models`, the binding's `options`, and `favicon = gateway.favicon`. That synth flows through `activate_provider` / `deactivate_provider` / reverse-derivation UNCHANGED. Editing a gateway re-activates its active bindings (mirrors `ProvidersPage.saveProvider`); deleting a gateway (or unbinding) `deactivate_*`s only the bindings that were actually active (marker + creds). A delete that can't clear a binding's live config (CLI running → DB locked) surfaces `toast.clearFailed` and **KEEPS the gateway** so it isn't silently lost while a CLI still points at its endpoint — mirrors `ProvidersPage.deleteProvider`; the retry is idempotent (already-cleaned bindings skip). When a standalone provider and a binding share identical creds (indistinguishable on disk), the per-CLI marker `active_provider_ids` (config.json) — honored only while its creds match the live snapshot — disambiguates which is "in use" via `resolveActiveProviderId`.
- **UI:** a separate **"Gateways" tab** in `ProvidersPage` (`view` state — `"providers" | "gateways"`, the tab value `"gateways"` is not a `CliApp`). `GatewaysPage` lists gateways (add/detect/bind via `GatewayEditor`, activate/deactivate per binding, delete). The per-CLI Providers list ALSO surfaces gateway bindings targeting that CLI as `ProviderCard`s with a `gatewayBadge` — **view + activate/test only, NO Edit/Delete** (those props are omitted, so `ProviderCard` hides the buttons; bindings are managed exclusively from the Gateways tab — by design). The synth provider carries `favicon = gateway.favicon` so the card shows the gateway's icon. `ProvidersPage.refreshActive` passes `[...providers, ...gatewaySynth]` so a gateway-activated CLI matches the synth id instead of reading "Unmanaged".
- **Tests:** `config::tests` (gateways roundtrip + providers↔gateways no-clobber), `providers::tests` (`parse_model_ids`), `provider-utils.test.ts` (`gatewayBaseForProtocol` incl. path-less/sub-path cases, `appProtocols`, `protocolForBinding`, `providerFromBinding`, `isGatewayList`). The HTTP probes themselves are network-bound (not unit-tested).
- **Tray** surfaces gateway bindings: each CLI submenu lists its bindings alongside standalone providers (the tray synthesizes them via `providers::gateway_providers` (read once, grouped per CLI) → `provider_from_binding`, the Rust mirror of the frontend `providerFromBinding`, and activates through the same `activate` / `read_active_state` path; only **installed** CLIs (`detect_install_snapshot`) get a submenu). **Known gap (not yet done):** `detect_gateway_apis` is best-effort (a permissive gateway can answer both OpenAI and Anthropic on `/v1/models`).

## Menu-bar tray (macOS system tray)

A native system-tray icon (the macOS menu bar) for quick provider switching, separate from the in-app Providers rail route. **Click model per OS:** on macOS (menu-bar item) left click opens the menu; on Windows/Linux the menu is on RIGHT click (`show_menu_on_left_click(false)`) and LEFT click (release) opens the app window — the platform convention. (Linux appindicator trays deliver no click events, so left-click-opens-window is effectively the Windows path.) Implemented in `src-tauri/src/tray.rs` and installed from `lib.rs` `setup()`. Requires the `tauri` features `tray-icon` + `image-png` (Cargo.toml).

**Menu shape** — "Open", then up to 5 recent session titles, then one submenu per CLI (the active choice shown inline on the first-level row; submenu lists Official + that login's saved accounts + the user's providers + this CLI's gateway bindings, with the active one checkmarked):

```
Open
─────────────────────
Fix the flaky stats test          ← up to 5 most-recent session titles
Refactor the gateway editor          (newest first; click → terminal + resume)
…
─────────────────────
New Session             ▸  termory  ▸  Claude Code  ← up to 5 recent project dirs,
                           chats    ▸  Codex           each a nested submenu of every
                           …           Gemini          INSTALLED CLI; pick one → terminal
                           ─────────                   opens in the dir + CLI launches fresh
                           Choose Folder… ▸ (CLIs)  ← native dir picker → CLI fresh there
…
─────────────────────
Claude Code · Official · 🟢 12% 5h · 🟡 78% Weekly · 🟢 $3 / $10 Credits  ▸  ☑ Official   ← quota (+ pay-as-you-go credits) inline on the FIRST-LEVEL row,
                           ─────────
                           ☐ Anthropic
                           ☐ OpenRouter
Codex · OpenRouter      ▸  ☐ Official
                           ─────────
                           ☑ a@example.com     ← saved official LOGINS (Codex only),
                           ☐ b@example.com ⚠      directly under Official
                           ─────────
                           ☑ OpenRouter
                           ☐ My Gateway        ← gateway bindings sit after the
Gemini · …              ▸  …                      standalone providers
OpenCode · Official     ▸  …
─────────────────────
Exit
```

- **Recent sessions + New session** — up to 5 most-recent session titles sit flat under "Open" (newest first by `updated_at`; Memory/Skill, slash-command and empty-id records excluded; label = title → snippet, truncated; a record with NEITHER is dropped from the list rather than rendered as a placeholder — several such rows are indistinguishable, and this menu exists to click the conversation you mean. Filtered on the recent-sessions mapping, NOT on `picked`, because that vector also feeds the New Session project list below and a project whose sessions happen to be untitled is still somewhere you work; filtered before `take` so the list stays at RECENT_LIMIT named rows instead of shrinking (`select_recent_state_drops_untitled_sessions` / `_keeps_a_snippet_only_session` / `_untitled_sessions_still_offer_their_project`)) — a single click (`tray:session:{source}:{id}` — content-addressed, looked up by identity so a watcher rebuild while the menu is open can't resume the WRONG session; missing → silent no-op) **launches a fresh OS terminal**, `cd`s into the session's cwd (when that dir exists) and resumes via **`terminal::resume_session(source, id, project)`** — the SAME entry point the Records / Favorites right-click "Resume in terminal" uses. Below the sessions one localized **"New Session" SUBMENU** lists the first 5 distinct project cwds in the same recency order (dedup ACROSS CLIs; empty-cwd records skipped) as NESTED submenus (cwd basename), each holding one row per INSTALLED CLI, ordered by which CLI was last used in THAT project (cli_recency — most likely choice on top), then the rest (flat header+rows grew too long) — picking a row (`tray:new:{cliKey}:{full project path}` — the path rides in the id, fully decoupled from RECENT; the `tray:newpick:` dispatch check must stay FIRST since it shares the prefix) calls `terminal::new_session(cli_source(cli), project)`: the chosen terminal opens in that cwd and that CLI launches fresh (any installed CLI, not just the ones with history there). A **"Choose Folder…"** tail submenu (`tray:newpick:{cliKey}`, localized `tray.chooseFolder`) opens the native directory picker (tauri-plugin-dialog `pick_folder`, async callback) and launches the picked CLI fresh in the chosen dir — works for brand-new directories and keeps New Session present with zero history (whole submenu skipped only when no CLI is installed). Both halves are computed by `select_recent_state` into `static RECENT: Mutex<RecentState>`, refreshed by `tray::refresh_recent(app, &records)` from the **existing** scans (the `scan_all_sessions` IPC + the watcher) — the tray never scans on its own, and the update is skipped when nothing changed. The refresh splices the dynamic region IN PLACE (`update_recent_region`: `Menu::remove_at`/`insert_items` over the cached root handle + region length in `RECENT_REGION`; region = items from `build_recent_region`, shared with build_menu, starting at the fixed `REGION_START` = 2 right after "Open"+separator) — a full `set_menu` rebuild CLOSES an open menu on macOS, while in-place splicing updates the open menu live (user requirement: refreshes apply immediately AND never dismiss the menu). Any splice failure falls back to a full rebuild. ONE deliberate exception to "never dismiss": an installed-set change (CLI installed/uninstalled since the menu was built) forces a full rebuild even from the menu-open path — the splice can't refresh the per-CLI submenus, and a one-time dismissal beats a permanently stale CLI list (see "Install/uninstall auto-refresh" below). ALL menu mutations (rebuilds, the splice, quota title set_text) are QUEUED on the main thread via `run_on_main_thread` — the queue serializes concurrent refreshers (watcher thread + the scan_all_sessions IPC) that would otherwise interleave remove/insert ops or write a stale menu handle back; a mutex was rejected (worker-holds-lock-waits-main vs main-waits-lock deadlock). (An earlier per-project-submenu design — project rows each holding new+sessions — was tried and rejected: it added a hover step to the most-frequent action and produced duplicate same-named rows.) `resume_session` builds the command via `session_launch_command` (mirrors the frontend `resumeCommandFor` — all four resume by id: `claude --resume <id>` / `codex resume <id>` / `opencode --session <id>` / `gemini --resume <id>`; the session id is charset-guarded `[A-Za-z0-9._-]` before interpolation — injection defense), reads the user's **Settings → Terminal** choice (`terminal` config key; empty / `"auto"` → the OS default), and calls `terminal::open(choice, project, cmd)`. `terminal::detect()` (the `detect_terminals` IPC) lists the mainstream terminals found installed per-OS (macOS: Terminal/iTerm/Ghostty/Alacritty/Kitty/WezTerm; Linux: GNOME/Konsole/XFCE/Alacritty/Kitty/WezTerm/xterm; Windows: Windows Terminal/PowerShell/cmd) — only macOS is verified, Linux/Windows best-effort.
  - **Cold-launch single-window (macOS GUI terminals).** Terminal.app and iTerm are activated via AppleScript (`terminal_app_script` / `iterm_script`), and a naive `activate` + `do script` / `create window` opens TWO windows when the app wasn't already running (the launch's default empty window plus the command's). The builders guard against this: already running → a fresh window; cold launch → reuse the window the launch creates (Terminal: `do script … in window 1`; iTerm: a delay-guarded `count of windows` check that writes into the launch window, else makes one). Asserted by `terminal::tests::{terminal_app_script,iterm_script}_reuses_launch_window_on_cold_start` — don't revert to the bare `activate`+`do script` form. The CLI-launched terminals (Ghostty via `open -na`, Alacritty/Kitty/WezTerm and ALL Linux/Windows terminals) spawn one window per invocation, so they don't have this issue.
- **Recent-session live work status (Claude-only)** — a currently-running Claude session's row shows its live status after the title: `{title} · Working` / `{title} · Needs input` (idle / not-running rows show just the title). **Plain text, no glyph** — a native macOS menu is an all-text list, so a colored dot would mean either an emoji (cheap) or a far-left icon column (breaks the otherwise icon-less menu); the word alone stays clean. Source is Claude's OWN native runtime file `~/.claude/sessions/<pid>.json` (respects `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR`) — the snapshot it writes for `claude ps`, NOT the transcript and NOT a hook (so Termory reads it in place). Verified against Claude source: `utils/concurrentSessions.ts:19` `SessionStatus = 'busy' | 'idle' | 'waiting'` (+ `waitingFor` detail), derived in `screens/REPL.tsx:1605`. `sessions::claude_work_statuses()` → `claude_work_statuses_in` reads the dir → `parse_claude_status_snapshot` maps `busy`→Busy, `waiting`(unless `waitingFor == "dialog open"`, the user-opened-UI case)→Waiting, and `idle`/terminal `state`/missing → no entry (prefers the richer `state` field — working/blocked — when present, else `status`+`waitingFor`). Files are filtered by Claude's exact filename guard `^\d+\.json$` (all-ASCII-digit stem; the `2026-…_notes.md`-as-pid bug, anthropics/claude-code#34210), a **PID-liveness probe** (`process_alive`: `libc::kill(pid, 0)` on unix via `[target.'cfg(unix)'.dependencies] libc`, `OpenProcess` on Windows via `[target.'cfg(windows)'.dependencies] windows-sys`, conservative `true` on exotic targets; mirrors Claude's `isProcessRunning`, so a crashed session's residual busy file isn't shown — mtime alone can't tell a long-busy live session from a crashed one), and a 24h-mtime **PID-reuse backstop** (`CLAUDE_STATUS_MAX_AGE`). Joined onto recent rows by `id == sessionId` (Claude rows only) in `tray::attach_work_statuses`; the FS read runs on `refresh_recent`'s caller thread (off the main thread), and `RecentSession.status` feeds `work_status_label` at render time in `build_recent_region`. **Refresh is event-driven, no timer:** a live status change rewrites the `<pid>.json` under the recursively-watched `~/.claude/`, so the watcher's `refresh_recent` reflects busy↔idle↔waiting promptly. A CRASH (kill -9) leaves the file untouched — no filesystem event — so the tray click (`on_tray_icon_event`) ALSO calls `tray::refresh_work_status`, which re-runs the liveness probe against the CACHED recent list (no session scan — only re-reads `~/.claude/sessions/` + re-attaches) and re-splices, clearing a crashed session's stale status on the next menu open. `refresh_recent` + `refresh_work_status` share `installed_cli_list` + `commit_recent` (store-if-changed → in-place splice → full-rebuild fallback, queued on the main thread). Status text is localized via the tray-labels push (`tray.statusBusy` / `tray.statusWaiting`). **Caveat:** the `status` field is written only when Claude's `BG_SESSIONS` feature is on (`REPL.tsx:1623`); with it off the file has no status → no work status surfaces (graceful degradation). Other CLIs have no equivalent live-status file, so this is Claude-only — and that's a hard limit, not a missing feature (cc-status is Claude-only for the same reason). **Codex (verified, don't re-investigate):** its live session state lives in the app-server **daemon, in-memory**, reachable only over an IPC socket (`app-server/src/thread_state.rs` — `Arc<CodexThread>` + listeners), NOT on disk; `~/.codex/sessions/` holds only rollout JSONL (history), the `threads` table in `state_5.sqlite` has NO status/active/pid column, and the one daemon `pid.rs` file is a single `{pid, processStartTime}` for the app-server process (not per-session). The only disk-derivable Codex signal is rollout-file mtime = "recently active", which can't distinguish busy/waiting/idle and reads identical for an idle-but-running session vs a closed one — misleading, so NOT used. Real Codex status would require speaking the daemon's IPC protocol (a live socket client, not a file read) — out of scope. **Gemini / OpenCode** likewise write no per-session run-status file. Tests: `sessions::tests::{parse_claude_status_snapshot_maps_running_states, claude_work_statuses_in_filters_by_filename_and_liveness}`, `tray::tests::{attach_work_statuses_joins_claude_rows_by_id_only, work_status_label_maps_states}`.
- **Quota inline title** — a quota-capable CLI's FIRST-LEVEL row appends the official-account quota after "Official" (`Claude Code · Official · 🟢 12% 5h · 🟡 78% Weekly`, percent right after the glyph; every window EXCEPT the two LEGACY per-model weeklies `seven_day_opus` / `seven_day_sonnet` (`TRAY_HIDDEN_TIERS` — that hide-set is exactly those two ids, kept only in case they return non-null; the API has since moved model-scoped limits into the `limits` array named by `scope.model.display_name`, so Claude's brand-name weeklies like "Fable" **DO** show on the tray — composed by `tray_tier_label` as `{period} · {model}` ("W · Fable") from the tier's `group` via `tray_group_label`, no longer a verbatim fall-through); `tray_tier_label` (card mirror: `tierLabels` in OfficialAccountsSection.tsx) renders `30_day` → localized "Monthly", other generated `{n}_hour`/`{n}_day` → "{n}h"/"{n}d", Gemini classes → brand "Pro"/"Flash"/"Flash Lite", a window carrying a `group` → `{period} · {model}` ("W · Fable", via `tray_group_label`), unknown ids raw; from `quota::fetch_quota`. `TrayQuota.tiers` holds `TrayTier { name, group, used }` — the API's own fields, NOT a pre-rendered label, so a language push relabels the cached quota instead of freezing the locale it was fetched under). Pay-as-you-go **credits** (Claude extra-usage / grok on-demand) append LAST as `{glyph} $used / $limit Credits` (`TrayCredits` populated from `quota.extra_usage` when `is_enabled`; rendered by `credits_label` + `format_credit_amount` — a no-Intl mirror of `formatCurrency` that divides MINOR units by `10^decimal_places`, drops `.00` on whole amounts, and suffixes non-USD codes; `Credits` label = localized `tray.credits`, "Credits" / 额度 / 額度; no cap → `$used Credits`), and grok's PREPAID balance after that as `$12.50 Balance` (`TrayQuota.balance` ← `quota.prepaid_balance`, `balance_label`, i18n `tray.balance`) — **with no glyph**, since every other segment's glyph reports a used/limit ratio and a balance has no limit to measure against. Each window carries a pressure emoji (`quota_glyph`: <75% 🟢, ≥75% 🟡, ≥90% 🔴 — thresholds are `quota::{WARN_PCT,CRIT_PCT}`, mirrored by `QUOTA_WARN_PCT`/`QUOTA_CRIT_PCT` in src/lib/quota-utils.ts for the app's ring); emoji because macOS menu text can't be colored. Suppressed while a custom provider is active — the quota belongs to the official login, so gluing it onto a custom provider's name would misread as that provider's usage. Cached per quota-capable CLI (`quota::SUPPORTED` — the backend's single support list; frontend mirror `QUOTA_SUPPORTED` in src/hooks/useQuotas.ts) in `static QUOTA: Mutex<Vec<(CliApp, TrayQuota)>>`; updated by `tray::refresh_quota` from THREE triggers (NO periodic polling): a one-shot warm-up at startup (`trigger_quota_refresh` in `setup()`), every tray-icon click (= menu open, via `on_tray_icon_event` — rate-limited per CLI to one fetch per 2 min (60s after a failure) via `QUOTA_LAST_FETCH`, which `refresh_quota` updates on EVERY completed fetch — including the Providers page's IPC — so page and tray share one rate limit; the fetch lands after the menu is showing; the changed numbers are applied IN PLACE via `Submenu::set_text` on cached `CLI_ROWS` handles (`update_cli_row_title` / `cli_row_title`) — a full `rebuild_menu`/`set_menu` here CLOSED the open menu on macOS), every COMPLETED `fetch_subscription_quota` IPC (success or failure — a Providers-page refresh updates the tray, the shared marker, and the not_found clearing with no extra network hit), and the filesystem watcher: a change to a CLI's credential file (`.credentials.json` → Claude, `.codex/auth.json` → Codex, `.gemini/oauth_creds.json` → Gemini; the path→CLI map is `quota::credential_cli_for_path`, single-sourced next to the credential readers — watcher.rs just delegates) calls `tray::force_quota_refresh` — bypasses the normal floors (10s burst-dedup only) so login/logout reflects near-instantly (Keychain-backed creds emit no file event; the 60s not_found retry is the fallback). Every completed fetch is ALSO pushed to the frontend via the `termory:quota-changed` event (payload = `SubscriptionQuota`; const `quota::QUOTA_CHANGED_EVENT`, mirrored in src/constants.ts) so an open Providers page reflects backend-initiated fetches without its own request. A `not_found` result (logged out — definitive, unlike transient failures) CLEARS that CLI's tray quota entry instead of keeping stale numbers; likewise a SUCCESSFUL fetch with no usable windows — logged in but with no active subscription, where `/api/oauth/usage` returns windows whose `utilization` is null/absent (verified against the official CLI: `services/api/usage.ts` types `utilization` as `number | null` and `Settings/Usage.tsx:218` shows "/usage is only available for subscription plans" precisely when no window is present) — clears the entry, so a cancelled-subscription account doesn't keep showing stale percentages. **UNLESS pay-as-you-go credits or a prepaid balance are present** — the empty-windows guard is `tiers.is_empty() && credits.is_none() && balance.is_none()`, so such an account keeps its entry and the tray still shows the `Credits` / `Balance` suffix even with no windows (matching the in-app card, which renders both off their own fields regardless of tiers). Other failures keep the last good numbers; the title update is skipped when unchanged. Quota changes never trigger a full rebuild while running (set_text only; full rebuilds still happen on provider mutations / recent-list changes / label pushes / installed-set changes).
- **The submenu MIRRORS the Providers page, single-sourced through `tray::CliProviders`** (LOCKED — the page is the reference for both what is listed and what a switch writes; `CliProviders::resolve` is built once per CLI and every branch of `build_menu` / `handle_menu_event` reads that one answer, so the menu and the decisions made off it can't disagree). Field-by-field mirror: `rows` = the page's `customProviders` ++ `gatewayBoundForApp` (standalone providers with `kind == Custom`, in library order, then this CLI's gateway bindings — the listed choices AND the candidate set the active id matches against); `standalone` = its `providersForApp` (EVERY kind, since this is the option-key STRIP SET handed to `activate` — narrowing it would leave a sibling's keys behind in the live config); `all` = its `allProvidersForApp` (standalone + gateway synths), handed to `deactivate` and Grok's `set_default`. A row's label is `provider_name()` = the name, else the localized `(unnamed)` placeholder (the page's `p.name || t("providers.unnamed")`) — a blank name would otherwise render an empty menu line. Tests: `tray::tests::{cli_providers_mirrors_the_page_lists, provider_name_falls_back_to_the_unnamed_placeholder}`.
- **Active state is reverse-derived** via `providers::read_active_state` (same as the Providers page) — Termory still stores no "active provider" pointer, only a marker RECORD (below). `CliProviders::active_choice` resolves it ONCE per CLI per build into `ActiveChoice { id, official }`, and the two fields answer DIFFERENT questions — do not collapse them:
  - **`id`** = the page's `effectiveActiveId`, driving the per-provider checkmarks and the switch-direction logic. The per-CLI `active_provider_ids` marker wins ONLY while the marked provider's `base_url` + masked `api_key` still match the live snapshot, else the reverse-derived `matched_provider_id` (`providers::resolve_active_provider_id`, the Rust mirror of `resolveActiveProviderId` — keep the two in sync). Multi-slot (OpenCode / Grok) SKIP the marker: their `matched_provider_id` comes from the live startup-default pointer, which carries the id already. **Without the marker the tray checkmarked the wrong row** whenever a standalone provider and a gateway binding shared one endpoint — field matching alone can't tell them apart, so it picked whichever came first and disagreed with the page.
  - **`official`** = `state.kind == ActiveKind::Official`, the page's `activeState.kind === "official"` — what drives ITS Official card's "in use" badge. It gates the Official ROW's checkmark, the inline `· Official` suffix, and the quota suffix. **`id.is_none()` is NOT the same question:** an `Unmanaged` live config (a third-party endpoint matching NO provider in the library — cc-switch, a hand-edit, an endpoint edited in the CLI's own config) also has no id, and reading that as Official ticked the Official row, titled the CLI `· Official`, and hung the official account's QUOTA off someone else's endpoint — the exact misread the custom-provider quota suppression exists to prevent. Under Unmanaged the page marks nothing as in use; the tray now matches, showing no checkmark, no quota, and a bare `Codex` title with no `· name` suffix. A `read_active_state` FAILURE claims nothing either (`ActiveChoice::default()`).
  - The Codex bucket-direction check deliberately uses `id.is_none()`, not `official` — that mirrors the page's own prompt conditions (`effectiveActiveId === null`), so an unmanaged config prompts on the way out to a custom provider exactly as the page does.
- **The tray WRITES the marker too** (`config::set_active_provider_marker`, the Rust mirror of the page's `markActive`): a successful switch records the clicked provider's id under `active_provider_ids` in config.json, Official removes the entry. Both entry points therefore leave identical state, and both reverse-derivations disambiguate identical-creds entries the same way. The `write_app_config` IPC rebuilds the menu when that key changes, because the page writes the marker AFTER the activate/deactivate IPC that already rebuilt — without it the tray would keep resolving with the PREVIOUS marker. The page re-reads the key on `providers-changed` (`refreshMarkers` → `invalidateConfigCache` + `getConfig`), since a backend write can't invalidate the frontend's module-level config cache.
- **Saved official logins (accounts) — Codex + Claude + Grok.** `accounts::tray_accounts(cli)` returns `[]` for every other CLI (`list_accounts` is display-only for Gemini), so no other submenu grows an account section. Rows sit DIRECTLY UNDER Official, separated by a rule and with no title row — they are that login's accounts, not more providers, and the Providers page likewise carries them on the Official card above the provider list. Label = `name · email` (either alone when only one exists, else the id); a `⚠` suffix marks `needs_relogin`. A row is DISABLED for `needs_relogin` (fixable only by re-authenticating on the page, so the click would just fail) or while an add-account flow is running for that CLI (the switch would be overwritten when the flow ends — the IPC and the page's button refuse for the same reason, and a native menu has nowhere to explain a dead click). **Both conditions live in ONE place, `tray::account_row_enabled`, read by the build path AND the in-place refresh** — the menu is built once then updated in place, so a row enabled by one and disabled by the other is a reachable, sticky state. **Starting a login rebuilds nothing**, so the tray click handler calls `refresh_accounts` alongside its quota and work-status refreshes; without it the menu would still be the one built before the login. **Whenever an account/provider rule changes, change it on BOTH surfaces (LOCKED)**: the page and the tray are two front ends over the same state, and a guard added to only one leaves the other as a silent way around it — that is how the switch guard first shipped, greying the page's button while the tray row still looked clickable and did nothing — the LIVE row stays enabled so the account in use doesn't render greyed-out next to its own checkmark; clicking it is a no-op, guarded in the handler exactly as the page guards it (`switchTo`: `if (account.active) return`). A click runs `accounts::switch_account` async (BOTH CLIs validate by refreshing the snapshot's tokens over the network first), then `mark_account_relogin(id, false)` on success + `force_quota_refresh` — on failure only CODEX is flagged from the tray/frontend; Claude flags itself BACKEND-side and only on AuthFailure (a string Err can't distinguish a dead RT from a locked-Keychain write error, and flagging a write error would trap a healthy row). Same split as the page's `switchTo`. **That completion updates the rows IN PLACE, never by rebuilding** (`tray::refresh_accounts` → `apply_account_rows` over the cached `ACCOUNT_ROWS` handles: `set_text` for the ⚠ suffix, `set_checked`, `set_enabled`) — the same technique as the quota suffix's `update_cli_row_title`, and for the same reason: `set_menu` closes an open menu on macOS, the switch lands SECONDS after the click (network token refresh), and reopening the menu to see whether it worked is precisely what a user does next — so a rebuild there dismissed the menu on *every* account switch, not as an edge case. It falls back to a full rebuild only when the cache can't express the change (no cached rows, or the account SET changed — added / removed / reordered); `account_row_label` is shared by the build and the refresh so one account can't render differently in the two paths. **The in-place path is gated on `cli_row_title_is_stale`** (`tray::settle_account_change`): it re-derives the row title (`cli_base_title`, shared with `build_menu`) and rebuilds when it differs, showing the truth rather than keeping the menu open. The switch REWRITES auth.json, which Codex's provider derivation also reads (`auth_mode` / `OPENAI_API_KEY` live there) — but `switch_codex` now carries those two fields over from the LIVE file (see "Account snapshots vs. provider switching" above), so an account switch no longer moves the Official/provider checkmarks and this check takes the in-place branch essentially always. It is KEPT as the guard for what it can't rule out: a provider moved by something else between the menu being built and the switch landing. (Before that fix, restoring a snapshot taken while a custom provider was active really did drag the provider along — the rebuild branch was the common case for it.) A FAILED switch leaves auth.json untouched, so it refreshes in place unconditionally. **Before switching it calls `accounts::auto_save_unsaved_live_account(cli)`**: switching OVERWRITES auth.json, and the page guards that loss with a warning in its confirm dialog ("the current login isn't saved") which a native menu row has nowhere to show — so it snapshots instead, which is what the warning is for. Idempotent, and the same call the `codex login` flow makes.
- **CLI display names** match the Providers page tabs (`CLI_APP_LABEL` in `src/constants.ts`) — keep `cli_label` in `tray.rs` in sync (it's "Gemini", not "Gemini CLI").
- **Static row labels are localized** (Open / Official / Exit). The tray is Rust (built in `setup()` before the frontend loads, so it can't read the i18n dicts), so the FRONTEND pushes the translated strings: an `App.tsx` effect (deps `[t, localeReady]`) calls the **`set_tray_labels({ labels })`** IPC on language load/change → `tray::set_labels` stores them in a `static TRAY_LABELS: Mutex<Option<TrayLabels>>` (English default) → `rebuild_menu`. The payload is ONE object deserialized into `tray::TrayLabels` (camelCase, every field required) — it was a dozen positional `String` params, where any reorder silently mislabelled the menu; name-matching makes a mismatch a compile/deserialize error instead. CLI / provider names stay untranslated (brand / user data). Keeps i18n single-sourced in the frontend dicts (no Rust translation table to drift). `tray.open` / `tray.official` / `tray.exit` / `tray.newSession` / `tray.chooseFolder` / `tray.statusBusy` / `tray.statusWaiting` / `tray.credits` / `tray.balance` keys, plus `providers.unnamed` for the nameless-provider placeholder (+ the quota row reuses `providers.quotaFiveHourShort` / `providers.quotaSevenDayShort` / `providers.quotaMonthlyShort` (30_day → "Monthly")). **The push is gated on `useI18n().ready`** — `<I18nProvider>` starts `ready=false`, flips it `true` (via `.finally`) once the saved `language` config has loaded, so the tray gets ONE push with the FINAL locale instead of first flashing the `navigator`-guessed one (the provider seeds from `navigator.language`, then the async config override wins; without the gate the tray briefly showed the system-guessed locale). `ready` defaults `true` in the no-provider context so isolated component tests are unaffected.
- **Click handler** reuses the same write helpers as the IPC commands (`activate` / `deactivate` / `set_default`), so the on-disk path is single-sourced, and `CliProviders::switch_to` writes exactly what the page's `performOfficial` / `performSetAsDefault` / `performActivateGateway` write for the same choice: Official → `deactivate(cli, all)`; a standalone row → `activate(p, standalone)`; a GATEWAY row → `activate(synth, [synth])` (itself as the whole strip set — the convention every GatewaysPage call uses). For the **multi-slot CLIs (OpenCode + Grok)** a click then also runs `set_default(p, all)` (the slot/entries must exist before it can be the default — `set_default` errors otherwise), so one click both enables the slot and sets it as default, matching the Providers page end state. Single-slot CLIs (Claude / Codex / Gemini) need only `activate`. A row naming a provider that's gone from the library rebuilds and returns — the native check item already lit itself, so every non-writing path must rebuild to discard that.
- **Codex switch handover (the "follow sessions?" prompt).** Codex buckets resume history by `model_provider`, so an official↔custom switch hides a project's prior sessions unless they're re-tagged, and the page ASKS which projects should follow (`CodexFollowDialog`) before switching — see "Codex 'follow sessions' on provider switch". A native menu can't host that dialog, so unless the user turned on Settings → "Keep all sessions on a Codex switch" (`config::codex_follow_all`, default OFF), a bucket-CHANGING Codex click defers to `spawn_codex_bucket_switch` on a blocking worker, which asks the SAME question the page asks first — does any project actually hold off-target sessions (`codex_follow_candidates`, the page's `maybePromptThenActivate` filter)? **No candidates → it applies the switch right there**, exactly like a non-Codex click, and NO window opens (the page behaves identically: with nothing to migrate it never prompts). **Candidates → it writes nothing**: parks the request in `static PENDING_SWITCH`, rebuilds (to discard the native self-toggle), shows the window via `run_on_main_thread` and emits `termory:tray-switch-request`. A candidate-listing FAILURE prompts — switching silently could drop a project's history from `codex resume`, while a needless prompt costs one dialog. The check has to run off the menu-event (main) thread because it opens sqlite, which is the whole reason this path is async. `App.tsx` claims it — NOT ProvidersPage, which only mounts on the Providers route, so a window sitting elsewhere would drop the switch — by calling `take_pending_tray_switch` on mount AND on the event (the tray shows the window then emits, so a cold frontend can miss the event; the IPC is take-once, so polling twice can't run it twice), routes to Providers and hands the request down. The page then runs its normal prompt → activate → migrate flow, so both entry points behave identically. Its `traySwitchStartedRef` holds the request OBJECT, not an `app:providerId` token — App mints a fresh object per claim, so repeating the very same switch (click Official, cancel, click Official again) runs, while a re-render of the one in-flight request is skipped. The direction comes from `CliProviders::active_id`, NOT the raw marker: after an external change the marker can claim "custom" while the live config is Official, and the bucket would really change while `was_official != to_official` read false. **With `codex_keep_all_sessions` ON** the tray switches immediately and `spawn_codex_follow_all` re-tags every project holding off-target sessions on a blocking worker (sqlite + 100+ MB rollout rewrites — never the menu-event thread); the page honours the same setting in `maybePromptThenActivate`, so it means the same thing wherever you switch from. Both the deferred path and the direct click end in `tray::apply_switch` — activate/deactivate → marker write → rebuild → `termory:providers-changed` — so every tray switch leaves identical state regardless of which route it took.
- **Menu is rebuilt** (`tray::rebuild_menu`) after every tray click and after the IPC commands whose writes the menu reflects, so checkmarks / inline titles / rows stay in sync: the provider-mutating five (`activate_provider` / `deactivate_provider` / `delete_provider` / `set_default_provider` / `write_app_providers`), `write_app_gateways` (its sibling — the tray lists each CLI's gateway BINDINGS, so adding / editing / unbinding changes the menu identically), `write_app_config` when the `active_provider_ids` or `sources` key actually changed, and every account mutation (`save_account` / `switch_account` / `delete_account` / `login_and_save_codex_account` / `cancel_codex_login` / `mark_account_relogin` — they move the account rows, their checkmark, or the ⚠ suffix).
- After a tray switch the handler emits **`termory:providers-changed`**; `ProvidersPage` listens for it and re-derives active state, so an open Providers page reflects a tray switch even when unfocused.
- **Open / Exit** are plain `MenuItem`s (not `PredefinedMenuItem::quit`) so macOS attaches no native item icon. Exit calls `app.exit(0)`. The separator BEFORE Exit is added only when there's middle content — `has_middle = region_len > 0 || !cli_rows.is_empty()` (captured before `cli_rows` moves into the cache). With an empty middle (no installed/enabled CLI AND no records) the Open separator and the Exit separator would otherwise sit adjacent as a double rule between Open and Exit; skipping the Exit one leaves a single `Open ─── Exit` divider.
- **Icon**: embedded `icons/tray-icon.png` (36×36 = 18pt @2x, the macOS standard), a pure-black template image of the three-card terminal "chip" from the app icon, optically centered. On **macOS** it's marked `icon_as_template(true)` so the bar themes it for light / dark; on **Windows / Linux** the tray instead uses the COLORED app icon (`default_window_icon`) — a monochrome template reads as a black blob in those system trays, which don't do menu-bar theming. `icons/tray-template.svg` is the vector source; the menu-bar size is governed by the whole image (tray-icon crate scales it to a fixed 18pt height), so the artwork's transparent margin controls how large it reads.

Only **installed** CLIs get a submenu — callers probe `detect_install_snapshot()` (the same probe as the Providers page's `detect_clis`; the snapshot = installed map + `codex_terminal`, see the `detect_codex_installs` bullet) and `build_menu` skips any CLI not found, so the tray lists only what you can actually switch. **Install/uninstall auto-refresh:** `static INSTALLED` caches the install snapshot the VISIBLE menu reflects — stored only AFTER a successful `set_menu` (`do_rebuild_menu_with`) / tray `install()`, never inside `build_menu` (an early store would let a failed rebuild claim the new set and permanently kill the staleness check; on failure the cache keeps the old set so the next refresh retries). `rebuild_if_installed_stale` (main-thread, the single owner of the compare→rebuild step) is fed by three probe paths, each probing on ITS OWN thread and passing the snapshot along so nothing re-probes on main: the watcher's install-detection branch (the same one that emits `cli-install-changed`) calls `tray::refresh_installed`, which RETURNS the probed snapshot so the same burst's rescan reuses it via `refresh_recent_with` (one PATH probe per burst, not two); the `detect_clis` IPC (Providers-page Recheck) hands its result to `tray::refresh_installed_with`; and the shared `commit_recent` path (fed by `refresh_recent` + the tray-click `refresh_work_status`) does the same compare, so an install missed by the watcher self-heals on the next menu open / scan. A stale snapshot (including a `codex_terminal` flip — the standalone CLI installed/removed while the desktop app stays) forces a FULL rebuild (not the recent-region splice — the splice can't refresh the per-CLI submenus; this is the documented exception to "never dismiss the menu" above). **Claude Desktop is covered too:** its install marker is the config dir itself (`claude_desktop::is_installed`), which can't be watched before it exists — so the watcher watches the PARENT dir(s) (`claude_desktop::install_watch_parents()`, which shares its source with `current_paths` so the dir watched is by construction the dir `is_installed` checks — on macOS the single `config_parent_dir()` helper, `~/Library/Application Support`; on Windows `windows_config_parents()`, whose FULL candidate list is watched plus the MSIX `Packages\` dir, non-recursive.
